of cuspidal Hilbert modular forms of weight
k
and level
U_{0}({\mathfrak{n}})
as in
[
H
, §2]
.

Let
f\in S_{k}(U_{0}({\mathfrak{n}}))
be a normalized newform. It is an eigenform for all Hecke operators
T({\mathfrak{a}})
for ideals
{\mathfrak{a}}\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}_{F}
(cf.
[
H
, §2]
). Fix an embedding
\iota_{p}:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
.
Let

\rho_{f}:G_{F}\to{\mathrm{Aut}}_{L}(V_{f})

be an associated two dimensional irreducible Galois representation over a
p
-adic local field
L
.
For any prime
\mathfrak{l}\nmid p{\mathfrak{n}}
of
F
, the Galois representation
V_{f}
is unramified at
\mathfrak{l}
and

\mathrm{Tr}(\rho_{f}({\mathrm{Frob}}_{\mathfrak{l}}))=\lambda_{f}(\mathfrak{l}),\quad\det(\rho_{f}({\mathrm{Frob}}_{\mathfrak{l}}))=N_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{l})^{k_{0}-1}

(cf.
[
H06
, Thm. 2.43]
).
Here
{\mathrm{Frob}}_{\mathfrak{l}}
is the geometric Frobenius at
{\mathfrak{l}}
,
N_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}
denotes the norm map,

L
is a sufficiently large finite extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
containing the valuation ring
\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}(v)\subseteq\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
as in
[
H
, p. 312]
and
the Hecke eigenvalues
\lambda_{f}({\mathfrak{a}})
.
Note that
\det_{L}(V_{f})\cong L(1-k_{0})
and so
V_{f}(k_{0}/2)
is symplectic self-dual.

Let
L(V_{f}(k_{0}/2),s)
denote the associated complex
L
-function, normalized to have center at
s=0
.

F.5.2.
Results

Proposition F.17
.

Let
F
be a totally real field and
p
an odd prime totally split in
F
.
Let
f\in S_{k}(U_{0}({\mathfrak{n}}))
be a Hilbert modular newform over
F
of weight
k\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}[I]
and
V_{f}
an associated
p
-adic
L
-representation of
G_{F}
as above.
Let
{\mathbb{F}}
denote the residue field of
{\mathcal{O}}_{L}
.
Suppose that
there exists
g\in S_{k^{\prime}}(U_{0}({\mathfrak{n}}^{\prime}))

for some
k^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}[I]
and an ideal
{\mathfrak{n}}^{\prime}\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}_{F}

satisfying the following.

a)

There exists an isomorphism of
{\mathbb{F}}[G_{F}]
-modules

\overline{T}_{f}(k_{0}/2)\cong\overline{T}_{g}(k^{\prime}_{0}/2)

for an
{\mathcal{O}}_{L}
-lattice
T_{g}
of
V_{g}
,
where
L
is possibly enlarged so that
V_{g}
is also defined over
L
.

b)

The
p
-parity Conjecture
F.2
is true for the symplectic self-dual
G_{F}
-representation
V_{g}(k^{\prime}_{0}/2)
.

Then the
p
-parity Conjecture
F.2
is true for

V_{f}(k_{0}/2)
, i.e.

{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=0}L(V_{f}(k_{0}/2),s)\equiv\dim_{L}H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(F,V_{f}(k_{0}/2))\ \mathrm{mod}\ 2.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of Theorem
F.16
.
∎

Theorem F.18
.

Let
f\in S_{k}(U_{0}({\mathfrak{n}}))
be a Hilbert modular newform over a totally real field
F
as in Proposition
F.17
.
Suppose that
the residual representation
\overline{T}_{f}
is irreducible.
Then the
p
-parity Conjecture
F.2
is true for

V_{f}(k_{0}/2)
, i.e.

{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=0}L(V_{f}(k_{0}/2),s)\equiv\dim_{L}H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(F,V_{f}(k_{0}/2))\ \mathrm{mod}\ 2.

Proof.

It suffices to show the existence of a Hilbert modular newform
g
as in Proposition
F.17
.

By Hida,
f
is congruent to a parallel weight two normalized
Hecke eigenform
h\in S_{2t,t}(U_{0}(p^{\alpha}{\mathfrak{n}}))
for some
\alpha\geq 0
(see
[
H
, Thm. 3.2]
and
[
H89
, Thm. 2.3]
), i.e.

\lambda_{h}(\mathfrak{l})\in{\mathcal{O}}_{L}
and

\lambda_{f}(\mathfrak{l})\equiv\lambda_{h}(\mathfrak{l})\bmod{\mathfrak{m}},\qquad(\mathfrak{l}\nmid p{\mathfrak{n}})

(F.20)

(see also
[
Sh
,
Oh
]
).
Here
S_{2t,t}(U_{0}(p^{\alpha}{\mathfrak{n}}))
denotes the space
S_{2t,t,I}(S(p^{\alpha});{\mathbb{C}})
in
[
H89
, p. 143]
with
S=U_{0}({\mathfrak{n}})
.
Note that
h
has Neben character
\epsilon^{2-k_{0}}

where
\epsilon:({\mathcal{O}}_{F}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})^{\times}\to{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
denotes composition of the norm map
({\mathcal{O}}_{F}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})^{\times}\to{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
and the Teichmüller character (cf.
[
H89
, p. 143]
).

Put

g=h\otimes\omega^{\frac{k_{0}}{2}-1}\in S_{2t,t}(U_{0}(p^{\alpha}{\mathfrak{n}})).

Since
\overline{T}_{f}
is irreducible and totally odd,
it is absolutely irreducible.
Then in view of (
F.20
), it follows that

\overline{T}_{f}(k_{0}/2)\simeq\overline{T}_{g}(1)

as
{\mathbb{F}}[G_{F}]
-modules.
As for the property b), the
p
-parity conjecture for the weight two Hilbert modular form
h
is due to
Nekovář
[
NekMRtame
, Thm. C]
, concluding the proof.
∎

Appendix G
Rubin-type conjectures

In this section we formulate and prove an analogoue of Rubin’s conjecture over ramified quadratic extensions of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, and a refinement of the original conjecture over the unramified quadratic extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}

(see Theorems
G.25
,
G.29
and
G.36
).
The approach is based on the local sign decomposition for a
symplectic self-dual family of Galois representations of
G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}

induced from conjugate symplectic self-dual characters over a quadratic extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.

Throughout, we fix an odd prime
p
.

G.1.
Epsilon constants

The aim of this subsection is to calculate epsilon constants of Weil–Deligne representations of
W_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
of rank two induced by characters
26
26

26

For primes
p>2
, any irreducible Weil–Deligne representation of rank two is induced by a character.
. Their variation in
p
-adic anticyclotomic families leads to a backdrop for Rubin-type conjectures.

Throughout this subsection, a representation means a Weil–Deligne representation, and in particular, with complex coefficients.

G.1.1.
Set-up

Let
F
be a finite field extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
and

K/F
a quadratic field extension.
Denote the associated quadratic character of
F^{\times}

by
\omega_{K/F}
.

As before, let
\omega_{1}
be the unramified character of the Weil group
W_{K}

mapping geometric Frobenius to
q^{-1}
for
q:=|{\mathcal{O}}_{K}/{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}|
.
We identify that
K^{\times}\cong W_{K}^{\rm ab}

by Artin reciprocity sending a uniformizer to a geometric Frobenius.

Definition G.1
.

A continuous character

\psi:K^{\times}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
is
conjugate symplectic self-dual with respect to
F
if

\psi|_{F^{\times}}=\omega_{K/F}\cdot\omega_{1}.

(G.1)

In the case
F={\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
we omit the expression ‘with respect to
F
’.

Lemma G.2
.

Let
\psi:K^{\times}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
be a continuous character.
Then
\mathrm{Ind}_{K/F}\psi
is symplectic self-dual of weight
-1

if and only if
\psi
is conjugate symplectic self-dual with respect to
F
.

Proof.

This is a special case of
[
GGP
, Lem. 3.5 (i)]
.
In our case, it
simply follows from the determinant formula for induced representations:

\det(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/F}\psi)=\omega_{K/F}\cdot\psi|_{F^{\times}}.

∎

For a character
\psi
of
K^{\times}
, let
a(\psi)
denote the exponent of its conductor, namely

a(\psi)=0
if
\psi
is unramified, and otherwise, it is the smallest positive
integer
m
such that
\psi
is trivial on units
\equiv 1\ \mathrm{mod}\ {\varpi^{m}}

for
\varpi
a uniformizer. We often refer to
a(\psi)
as the conductor of
\psi
.

In the rest of the subsection we suppose that

F={\mathbb{Q}}_{p}

and consider self-dual epsilon constants as in §
2.5
.

G.1.2.
The unramified case.

The main result of this subsection is Proposition
G.4
.

Let
K
denote the unramified quadratic extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. Let
\delta\in K
be a
p
-adic unit such that
K={\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(\delta)
and
\delta^{2}\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.

Lemma G.3
.

i)

There is a unique unramified conjugate symplectic self-dual character
\phi_{K}
of
K^{\times}
.

ii)

Any conjugate symplectic self-dual character of
K^{\times}
is of the form
\phi_{K}\chi
, where

\chi
is a character of
K^{\times}
such that
\chi|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}=1
.

Proof.

Let
\psi
be a conjugate symplectic self-dual character of
K^{\times}
.
By (
G.1
), we have
\psi(p)=-p^{-1}
and so part i) follows.
In turn, it implies part ii).
∎

Proposition G.4
.

Let
\phi_{K}
and
\chi
be characters of
K^{\times}
as in Lemma
G.3
.
Then we have

\varepsilon(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\phi_{K}\chi))=\chi(\delta)(-1)^{a(\chi)}.

In particular, if the order of
\chi
is odd, then
\varepsilon(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\phi_{K}\chi))=(-1)^{a(\chi)}
.

Proof.

In the following the notation is as in §
2.5.1
.

Let
\xi
be a non-trivial additive character of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
with
n(\xi)=0
and put

\xi_{K}:=\xi\circ{\rm Tr}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
. Let
dx
be a Haar measure on
K

which is self-dual with respect to
\xi_{K}
.
Then,
n(\xi_{K})=0
and
\int_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}dx=1
.
Since
K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
and
\phi_{K}
are both unramified, we have

\varepsilon(\phi_{K},\xi_{K},dx)=\varepsilon(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi_{K})=1
. Hence, we have

\varepsilon(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\phi_{K}\chi))=\frac{\varepsilon(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi_{K}\chi)}{\varepsilon(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi_{K})}=\frac{\varepsilon(\phi_{K}\chi,\xi_{K},dx)}{\varepsilon(\phi_{K},\xi_{K},dx)}=\varepsilon(\phi_{K}\chi,\xi_{K},dx).

(G.2)

Note that

[\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi_{K}\chi]-[\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi_{K}]
is
a virtual representation of degree
0
(cf.
[
T
, (3.4.8)]
).

For Weil–Deligne representations
V
and
W
with
W
unramified, recall that

\varepsilon(V\otimes W,\xi,dx)=\varepsilon(V,\xi,dx)^{\mathrm{dim}\,W}\cdot\det W(\varpi)^{a(V)+n(\xi)\dim V}

(G.3)

(cf.
[
T
, (3.4.6)]
).
Together with Fröhlich–Queyrut
[
FQ
, Thm. 3]
, we have

\varepsilon(\phi_{K}\chi,\xi_{K},dx)=\varepsilon(\chi,\xi_{K},dx)\cdot\phi_{K}(p)^{a(\chi)}\equiv\chi(\delta)(-1)^{a(\chi)}\;\ \mathrm{mod}\ ^{\!\!\!\times}\,\mathbb{R}_{>0}.

Hence, the assertion follows from (
G.2
).
∎

G.1.3.
The ramified case.

The main result of this subsection is Proposition
G.7
.

Let
K
denote a ramified quadratic extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.
Since
p
is odd,
K
has two possibilities.
Let
\delta\in K
be a uniformizer such that
K={\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(\delta)
and
\delta^{2}\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.
Let
\xi
be a non-trivial additive character
of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
such that
n(\xi)=0
.
Then
\xi_{K}:=\xi\circ\mathrm{Tr}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
is an additive character of
K
of conductor
1
.

Any conjugate self-dual character of
K^{\times}
is ramified since so is

\omega_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
.

Lemma G.5
.

i)

There are exactly two
conjugate symplectic self-dual characters
\psi
of
K^{\times}
of conductor one. More precisely, they depend on the choice of a square root

\sqrt{p^{*}}
of

p^{*}:=\left(\frac{-1}{p}\right)p
, and are characterized by

\psi(\delta)=\sqrt{p^{*}}^{-1}.

In particular, their ratio equals the unramified quadratic character of
K^{\times}
.

ii)

Any conjugate symplectic self-dual character of
K^{\times}
is of the form
\phi_{K}\chi
for

\chi
a character of
K^{\times}
such that
\chi|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}=1
.

iii)

For any
\chi
as above,
the conductor
a(\chi)
is even.

Proof.

In view of (
G.1
), we have

\psi(\delta^{2})=\left(\frac{-1}{p}\right)p^{-1}

and

\psi|_{\mu_{p-1}}
is uniquely determined. Thus, part i) follows. Part ii) is immediate.

As for iii), note that
\chi|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}=1
, and
\chi(1+\delta^{a(\chi)-1})
is a primitive
p
-th root of unity.
∎

Our formula for epsilon constants will involve the following Gauss-like sum.

Definition G.6
.

For a ramified character of
K^{\times}
with
\chi|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}=1
,
define

G_{\chi,\delta}:=\sum_{a\in{\mathbb{F}}_{p}^{\times}}\left(\frac{a}{p}\right)\chi(1+\delta^{a(\chi)-1})^{a}.

Proposition G.7
.

Let
p
be an odd prime and
K
a ramified quadratic extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.
Let
\phi_{K}
be a conjugate symplectic self-dual character of
K^{\times}
of conductor one.

i)

We have

\varepsilon(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi_{K})=\omega_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(-2).

ii)

Let
\chi
be a ramified character of
K^{\times}
such that
\chi|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}=1
. Then

\displaystyle\varepsilon(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi_{K}\chi)

\displaystyle=\omega_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(-2)\cdot\phi_{K}\chi(\delta)\cdot\left(\frac{-1}{p}\right)^{\frac{a(\chi)}{2}}G_{\chi,\delta}

\displaystyle=\omega_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(p)\phi_{K}\chi(\delta)\left(\frac{-1}{p}\right)^{\frac{a(\chi)}{2}}\left(\frac{\overline{v}_{\chi}}{p}\right)\sum_{a\in{\mathbb{F}}_{p}^{\times}}\left(\frac{a}{p}\right)\xi(\frac{a}{p}),

where
v_{\chi}\in\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
is an element such that

\chi(1+\delta^{a(\chi)-1}a)=\xi_{K}({v_{\chi}^{-1}a}/{\delta^{2}})\quad\text{for $a\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}$}

and
\overline{v}_{\chi}
denotes its image in the residue field.

Proof.

In the following, the notation for epsilon constants is as in §
2.5
.

Let
\xi
be a non-trivial additive character of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
with
n(\xi)=0
as above, and
dx_{\xi}
the self-dual Haar measure on
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
with respect to
\xi
.
Then
\xi_{K}:=\xi\circ\mathrm{Tr}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
is an additive character of
K
with
n(\xi_{K})=1
and let
dx
be a Haar measure on
K
.

For any character
\eta
of
K^{\times}
with
\eta|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}=1
, we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi_{K}\eta,\xi,dx_{\xi})

\displaystyle=\varepsilon(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}1,\xi,dx_{\xi})\cdot\frac{\varepsilon(\phi_{K}\eta,\xi_{K},dx)}{\varepsilon(1,\xi_{K},dx)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon(1,\xi,dx_{\xi})}{\varepsilon(1,\xi_{K},dx)}\cdot\varepsilon(\omega_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}},\xi,dx_{\xi})\varepsilon(\phi_{K}\eta,\xi_{K},dx),

(G.4)

where
the second equality follows from the decomposition
\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}1=1\oplus\omega_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
.
Note that
\varepsilon(\omega_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}},\xi,dx_{\xi})
is a quadratic Gauss sum by definition.

We have

\displaystyle\varepsilon(\phi_{K},\xi_{K},dx)

\displaystyle=\int_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}}\phi_{K}^{-1}(x)\xi_{K}(x)dx

\displaystyle=p\phi_{K}(p)\sum_{a\in{\mathbb{F}}_{p}^{\times}}\phi_{K}(a)^{-1}\xi_{K}(a/p)\int_{1+{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}}dx

\displaystyle=\omega_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(2p)\sum_{a\in{\mathbb{F}}_{p}^{\times}}\omega_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(a)\xi(a/p)\int_{1+{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}}dx.

The latter equals
\omega_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(2)\varepsilon(\omega_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}},\xi,dx_{\xi})
up to a positive real number.
Hence, substituting
\eta=1
in
(
G.1.3
), we have

\varepsilon(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi_{K})=\omega_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(2)\frac{\varepsilon(\omega_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}},\xi,dx_{\xi})^{2}}{|\varepsilon(\omega_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}},\xi,dx_{\xi})|^{2}}=\omega_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(-2).

Now we consider part ii).
In the following
for a Weil–Deligne representation
W
of
W_{K}
we let

W(\rho,\xi_{K})
denote the root number
W(\rho,\xi_{K}):=\varepsilon(\rho,\xi_{K},dx)/|\varepsilon(\rho,\xi_{K},dx)|
, which is independent of the choice of the Haar measure
dx
.
Moreover for
a,b\in{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
we write
a\sim b
if
ab^{-1}\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\cdot\mu_{p^{\infty}}
.

By Lamprecht’s formula
[
TLC
, Prop. 1]
, we have

W(\phi_{K}\chi,\xi_{K})\sim(\phi_{K}\chi)(c_{\chi})\xi_{K}(c^{-1}_{\chi}).

Here
c_{\chi}\in K^{\times}
is an element with
v_{K}(c_{\chi})=a(\chi)+n(\xi_{K})=a(\chi)+1
so that

\chi(1+y)=\xi_{K}(c^{-1}_{\chi}y)

(G.5)

for any
y
satisfying
v_{K}(y)\geq a(\chi)/2
, where
v_{K}
is the normalized valuation of
K
with
v_{K}(\delta)=1
.

Since
c_{\chi}
has odd valuation,
\chi(c_{\chi}\delta^{-1})
is a
p
-power root of unity with
p\not=2
.
Hence, in view of (
G.5
), it follows that

W(\phi_{K}\chi,\xi_{K})\sim\phi_{K}(c_{\chi})\chi(\delta).

Write
c_{\chi}=\delta^{a(\chi)+1}v_{\chi}
for
v_{\chi}\in\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
.
Then for
a\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
, we have

\chi(1+\delta^{a(\chi)-1}a)=\xi_{K}({v_{\chi}^{-1}a}/{\delta^{2}})=\xi(u{v_{\chi}^{-1}a}/{p})\quad\text{}

for
u:=2p/\delta^{2}
and

W(\phi_{K}\chi,\xi_{K})\sim\phi_{K}(c_{\chi})\chi(\delta)\sim\phi_{K}\chi(\delta)\left(\frac{-1}{p}\right)^{\frac{a(\chi)}{2}}\left(\frac{\overline{v}_{\chi}}{p}\right).

As for the latter relation, we have used the fact
\phi_{K}(-\delta^{2})\sim 1
since

-\delta^{2}\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
is a norm from
K
.

Hence, by (
G.1.3
), it follows that

\displaystyle\varepsilon(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi_{K}\chi)

\displaystyle=\omega_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(p)\phi_{K}\chi(\delta)\left(\frac{-1}{p}\right)^{\frac{a(\chi)}{2}}\left(\frac{\overline{v}_{\chi}}{p}\right)\sum_{a\in{\mathbb{F}}_{p}^{\times}}\left(\frac{a}{p}\right)\xi(a/p).

\displaystyle=\omega_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(-2)\phi_{K}\chi(\delta)\left(\frac{-1}{p}\right)^{\frac{a(\chi)}{2}}\sum_{a\in{\mathbb{F}}_{p}^{\times}}\left(\frac{a}{p}\right)\chi(1+\delta^{a(\chi)-1}a).

∎

Corollary G.8
.

In the setting of Proposition
G.7
,
suppose that the order of
\chi
is a power of
p
.

i)

For
b\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
, we have

\varepsilon(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi_{K}\chi^{b})=\left(\frac{b}{p}\right)\varepsilon(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi_{K}\chi)

ii)

If
\chi^{p}\not=1
, then

\varepsilon(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi_{K}\chi^{-\delta^{2}})=\varepsilon(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi_{K}\chi)

Proof.

This is a consequence of Proposition
G.7
.

For illustration, we consider part ii). Put
\chi^{\prime}:=\chi^{-\delta^{2}}
.
Then
a(\chi)=a(\chi^{\prime})+2
and

\chi(1+\delta^{a(\chi)-1})=\chi(1-\delta^{a(\chi^{\prime})-1})^{-\delta^{2}}=\chi^{\prime}(1-\delta^{a(\chi^{\prime})-1}).

Hence
G_{\chi,\delta}=\left(\frac{-1}{p}\right)G_{\chi^{\prime},\delta}
and the assertion follows
from Proposition
G.7
since
\chi(\delta)=1
.
∎

G.2.
Conjugate symplectic self-dual characters

In this subsection we explicitly describe conjugate symplectic self-dual
p
-adic Galois characters over quadratic extensions of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.

G.2.1.
Notions

Fix an algebraic closure
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.

Let
F
be a finite extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. Recall that
\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}:G_{F}\rightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
denotes the cyclotomic character, which we view as a character of
F^{\times}
via Artin reciprocity map of local class field theory.
Let
K/F
be a quadratic field extension and
denote the associated quadratic character of
F^{\times}
by
\omega_{K/F}
.

Definition G.9
.

A continuous character

\psi:G_{K}\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}
is
conjugate symplectic self-dual with respect to
F
if

\psi|_{F^{\times}}=\omega_{K/F}\cdot\chi_{{\mathrm{cyc}}}

(G.6)

where we view
\psi
as a character of
K^{\times}
via Artin reciprocity map.

Note that a
p
-adic character of
K^{\times}
satisfying (
G.6
) extends uniquely to
a continuous character of
G_{K}
via the Artin reciprocity map.
In this way we regard
such characters of
K^{\times}
as Galois characters.

Lemma G.10
.

Let
\psi:G_{K}\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}
be a continuous character.
Then
\mathrm{Ind}_{K/F}\psi
is symplectic self-dual
if and only if
\psi
is conjugate symplectic self-dual with respect to
F
.

Proof.

We proceed as in the proof of Lemma
G.2
.
∎

In the rest of this subsection let
R
be a commutative profinite ring.

Definition G.11
.

A continuous character
\psi:G_{K}\rightarrow R^{\times}
is
anticyclotomic with trivial
central character with respect to
F
if

\psi(cgc^{-1})=\psi(g^{-1})\text{ and }\psi(c^{2})=1

for any
g\in G_{K}
and
an extension
c\in G_{F}
of the non-trivial element of
\mathrm{Gal}(K/F)
.

When
F={\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, we omit the expression ‘with respect to
F
’.

Lemma G.12
.

i)

Definition
G.11
does not depend on the choice of an extension
c\in G_{F}
of the non-trivial element of
{\mathrm{Gal}}(K/F)
.

ii)

A character
\psi:G_{K}\rightarrow R^{\times}
is anticyclotomic with trivial
central character with respect to
F
if and only if

\psi|_{F^{\times}}=1
.

Proof.

Let
c_{1}
be another extension and put
d=cc_{1}^{-1}
. Then for
g\in G_{K}
,

\psi(c_{1}gc_{1}^{-1})=\psi(d)\psi(c_{1}gc_{1}^{-1})\psi(d)^{-1}=\psi(cgc^{-1})=\psi(g^{-1}).

Since
\psi(cdc^{-1})=\psi(d^{-1})
and
\phi(c^{2})=1
, we have
\psi(c_{1}^{-1}c^{-1})=\psi(c_{1}c^{-1})
.
This implies that

\psi(c_{1}^{2})=\psi(c_{1}c^{-1})\psi(cc_{1})=\psi(cc_{1})\psi(c_{1}c^{-1})=1.

The restriction to
F^{\times}
corresponds to the transfer map
t:G_{F}^{\rm{ab}}\rightarrow G_{K}^{\rm{ab}}

under the Artin reciprocity. Recall that
t(g)=g\cdot(c^{-1}gc)
if
g\in G_{K}^{\rm{ab}}
and

t(g)=c^{-1}g^{2}c=(c^{-1}gc)^{2}
if
g\in G_{F}^{\rm{ab}}\setminus G_{K}^{\rm{ab}}
.
The assertion ii) follows from this and i).
∎

Definition G.13
.

Let
K/F
be a quadratic field extension as above.
An abelian field extension
L
of
K
is a generalized dihedral extension of
F
if

i)

L
is Galois over
F
and,

ii)

There exists an element
c\in\mathrm{Gal}(L/F)

of order two extending the non-trivial element of
\mathrm{Gal}(K/F)
such that

\mathrm{Gal}(L/F)=\mathrm{Gal}(L/K)\rtimes\langle c\rangle

satisfying
cgc^{-1}=g^{-1}
for any
g\in\mathrm{Gal}(L/K)
.

By definition, any continuous character
\psi:G_{K}\rightarrow R^{\times}
factoring
through a generalized dihedral extension of
F

is anticyclotomic with trivial
central character with respect to
F
.
An example is provided by characters factoring through the anticyclotomic
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-extension of a quadratic extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.

The following preliminary will be used in some of our later arguments.

Lemma G.14
.

Let
p
be an odd prime and let
K
be a quadratic field extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.
Let
\Gamma
be the Galois group of the anticyclotomic
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-extension of
K
.

i)

Let
\pi
be a uniformizer of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
Then the image of

(1+\pi\mathcal{O}_{K})/(1+p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\rightarrow(1+\pi{\mathcal{O}}_{K})_{\mathrm{free}}:=(1+\pi{\mathcal{O}}_{K})/(1+\pi{\mathcal{O}}_{K})_{{\mathrm{tor}}},\quad\alpha\mapsto\alpha/c(\alpha)

is isomorphic to
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
.
Moreover,
1+\pi O_{K}
is free as a group if
K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
is unramified or
K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
is ramified and
p\not=3
.
In the excluded case, the torsion part of
1+\pi{\mathcal{O}}_{K}
is isomorphic to
\mu_{3}\cap K^{\times}
.

ii)

If
K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
is unramified,
then a character
\psi:G_{K}\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}
factors
through
\Gamma

if and only
if
\psi|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}=1
and
\psi|_{K^{\times}_{\rm{tor}}}=1
.

iii)

If
K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
is ramified, then a character
\psi:G_{K}\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}
factors
through
\Gamma

if and only
if
\psi|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}=\psi|_{\mu_{p}\cap K^{\times}}=\psi(\delta)=1
,
where
\delta
is a uniformizer of
K^{\times}
such that
\delta^{2}\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}.

Proof.

If
K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
is unramified or
K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
is ramified and
p\not=3
, then
1+\pi\mathcal{O}_{K}
is torsion-free
since the exponential map is defined on
\pi\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
In the excluded case note that

1+\pi\mathcal{O}_{K}=(\mu_{3}\cap K)\times(1+\pi^{2}\mathcal{O}_{K}),

and
1+\pi^{2}\mathcal{O}_{K}
is free by the same argument.

As for part iii), it is a consequence of the fact that the natural exact sequence

0\to(1+\pi{\mathcal{O}}_{K})/(1+p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\to K^{\times}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}\to{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}\to 0

splits uniquely such that
\delta\in K^{\times}
is the lift of
1\in{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
.
∎

G.2.2.
The unramified case

In this subsection
K
denotes the unramified quadratic extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.

Lemma G.15
.

Let
\mathscr{F}_{\pi}
be the Lubin–Tate formal group over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
for the uniformizing parameter
\pi
of
K
. Then
the associated Lubin–Tate character

\phi_{\pi}:G_{K}\rightarrow{\mathrm{Aut}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}T_{\pi}\mathscr{F}_{\pi}

is conjugate symplectic self-dual
if and only if
\pi=-p
.

Proof.

Note that
\phi_{\pi}(\pi)=1
and
\phi_{\pi}(u)=u
for
u\in\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
.
Then
\phi_{\pi}
is conjugate symplectic self-dual if and only if

\phi_{\pi}(p)=-1
.
The assertion follows from this.
∎

Remark G.16
.

The lemma recovers the well-known fact that
the formal group of a CM elliptic curve defined over
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
with good supersingular reduction at
p

is the Lubin–Tate formal group of height for the uniformizing parameter
-p
.

In view of the lemma, we let
\pi=-p
henceforth.
Let
\phi^{*}_{\pi}
denote the character
\phi_{\pi}\circ c
for
c\in\mathrm{Gal}(K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
the non-trivial element and

\psi_{\pi}:=\frac{\phi_{\pi}}{\phi^{*}_{\pi}}.

Lemma G.17
.

A
p
-adic character of
K^{\times}
is conjugate symplectic self-dual and de Rham if and only if
it is of the form
\phi_{\pi}\psi_{\pi}^{k}\chi

where
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\chi
a finite order character of
K^{\times}
with
\chi|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}=1
.

Proof.

Any de Rham character
\psi
of
G_{K}
is of the form
\phi_{\pi}^{a}\psi_{\pi}^{b}\mu\chi
,
where
a,b\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
\mu
is an unramified character and
\chi
a finite order character
(cf.
[
26
, App. B]
).

Considering the value of such a conjugate symplectic self-dual
\psi
at
p
, it follows that
\mu
is of finite order. So we may assume that
\mu=1
. Then,

\omega_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}=\psi|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}=\omega_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}^{a}\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}^{a}\chi|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}.

Hence,
a=1
and
\chi|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}=1
.
∎

Proposition G.18
.

Let
\psi
be a conjugate symplectic self-dual
p
-adic character

\psi=\phi_{\pi}\psi_{\pi}^{k}\chi:K^{\times}\rightarrow L^{\times}

for
L
a finite extension of
K
,
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\chi
a finite order character with
\chi|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}=1
.
Pick an embedding
\tau:L\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
and an isomorphism
\iota:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}\cong{\mathbb{C}}
.
Then the Weil–Deligne representation
\mathrm{WD}(\psi)_{\iota\circ\tau}
associated to
\psi
is the character
\phi_{K}\chi
, where

\phi_{K}
is the unique unramified conjugate symplectic self-dual Weil-Deligne character of
K^{\times}

and
\chi
is regarded as a
{\mathbb{C}}
-valued character via the embedding
\iota\circ\tau:L\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}
.

Proof.

Since
\phi_{\pi}\psi_{\pi}^{k}
is crystalline, it corresponds to
\phi_{K}
, the unique unramified
conjugate symplectic self-dual character. Hence, the proposition follows by noting that
the functor associating a
p
-adic potentially semistable Galois representation to
a Weil–Deligne representation is compatible with tensor product.

In the following we describe an alternate explicit argument.

We may assume that
\psi\in\{\phi_{\pi},\phi_{\pi}^{*},\psi_{\pi}\}
.
First we consider the case
\psi\in\{\phi_{\pi},\phi^{*}_{\pi}\}
.
Let
D(\psi)=D_{\mathrm{pst}}(\psi)
be the associated filtered
(\varphi,N,G_{K})
-module.
It is a
K^{\rm{un}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}L
-module of rank one.
Since
\psi
is crystalline, note that

D(\psi)=K^{\rm{un}}\otimes_{K}D
where
D:=D_{\rm{crys}}(\psi)

is a
K\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}L
-module of rank one with
N=0
. For
\psi=\phi_{\pi}
, the operator

\varphi^{2}
acts by
\pi^{-1}
on
D
, and
for
\psi=\phi^{*}_{\pi}
, it also
acts by
\pi/p^{2}=\pi^{-1}

since
\phi_{\pi}\cdot\phi_{\pi}^{*}
is the cyclotomic character over
K
.
In particular, for
\psi=\psi_{\pi}
, the operator
\varphi^{2}
acts trivially.

Now we consider the case
\psi=\phi_{\pi}\psi_{\pi}^{k}
.
Since the
G_{K}
-action on
D
is trivial,
the Weil group acts on the associated Weil–Deligne representation by

g\cdot v:=g\circ\varphi^{fv(g)}v=g\circ\varphi^{2v(g)}v=\pi^{-v(g)}v,

which coincides with
\phi_{K}
.
∎

G.2.3.
The ramified case

In this subsection
K
denotes a ramified quadratic extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.
Let
\delta\in K
be a uniformizer such that
\delta^{2}\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.

Let
\phi_{\delta}
be the Lubin–Tate character with parameter
\delta
.
Being crystalline, it is not conjugate symplectic self-dual, however its certain twist is as we now introduce.
Let
\theta
be a tamely ramified
p
-adic character of
K^{\times}
such that

\theta(\delta^{2})=\left(\frac{-1}{p}\right)\cdot\frac{\delta^{2}}{p},\quad\theta|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}}=\omega_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}}.

Depending on the choice of a square root of
p^{*}:=\left(\frac{-1}{p}\right)p
,
there are exactly two such characters
\theta
characterized by
\theta(\delta)=\delta/\sqrt{p^{*}}
.
Put

\phi_{\theta}=\phi_{\delta}\cdot\theta,\quad\psi_{\theta}=\frac{\phi_{\theta}}{\phi_{\theta}\circ c}

for
c\in{\mathrm{Gal}}(K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
the non-trivial element.

Lemma G.19
.

i)

The
p
-adic character
\phi_{\theta}
is conjugate symplectic self-dual.

ii)

A
p
-adic character of
G_{K}
is conjugate symplectic self-dual and de Rham if and only if
it is of the form
\phi_{\theta}\psi^{k}_{\theta}\chi

where
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\chi
a finite order character with
\chi|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}=1
.

Proof.

Part i) follows directly from the definition.

As for ii),
any de Rham character
\psi
of
G_{K}
is of the form
\phi_{\theta}^{a}\psi_{\theta}^{b}\mu\chi
,
where
a,b\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
\mu
an unramified character and
\chi
a finite order character.
Using it, one may proceed as in the proof of Lemma
G.17
.
∎

Proposition G.20
.

Let
\psi
be a conjugate symplectic self-dual
p
-adic character

\psi=\phi_{\theta}\psi_{\theta}^{k}\chi:G_{K}\rightarrow L^{\times}

where
L
is a finite extension of
K
,
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\chi
a finite character with
\chi|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}=1
.
Pick an embedding
\tau:L\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
and an isomorphism
\iota:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}\cong{\mathbb{C}}
.
Then the Weil–Deligne representation
\mathrm{WD}(\psi)_{\iota\circ\tau}
associated to
\psi
is the character
\psi_{K}\chi
, where

\psi_{K}
is the unique conjugate symplectic self-dual character of
K^{\times}
of conductor
1

with

\psi_{K}(\delta)=\iota\circ\tau(\psi(\delta)/\delta)=(-1)^{k}\left(\frac{-1}{p}\right)^{k}\iota\circ\tau(\theta(\delta)/\delta)

as in Lemma
G.5
i)
and
\eta
is regarded as a
{\mathbb{C}}
-valued character via the embedding
\iota\circ\tau
.

Proof.

It suffices to
calculate
WD(\rho)_{\iota\circ\tau}
for
\rho
being
\phi_{\delta}
, an unramified character or a finite character, for which one may
proceed as in the proof of Lemma
G.18
.
∎

G.3.
Rubin-type conjectures

G.3.1.
Set-up

As before, fix an algebraic closure
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}

and finite extensions of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
are taken in
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
.

Let
K
be a quadratic extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
and
L
a finite extension of
K
.
Let

\phi:G_{K}\rightarrow L^{\times}

be a de Rham conjugate symplectic self-dual character
with Hodge--Tate
27
27

27

precisely, the Hodge–Tate weights of
\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi
weights
(k+1,-k)
for
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
.
For example, we may take
\phi=\phi_{\pi}\psi_{\pi}^{k}
as in Lemma
G.15
if
K
is unramified,
and
\phi=\phi_{\theta}\psi_{\theta}^{k}
as in Lemma
G.19
if
K
is ramified.
Let
T_{\phi}
be the free
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-module of rank one on which
G_{K}
acts by
\phi
.

Let
K_{\infty}
denote the anticyclotomic
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-extension of
K
with Galois group
\Gamma
,
namely the unique
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-extension of
K
which is generalized dihedral over
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. Let
\Lambda
denote the Iwasawa algebra
\mathcal{O}_{L}[\![\Gamma]\!]
.
We denote by
K_{n}
the
n
-th layer.

We regard a character
\chi:\Gamma\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\times}_{p}

as a character of
G_{K}
via the natural map

G_{K}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gal}(K_{\infty}/K)=\Gamma
.

Definition G.21
.

i)

For an integer
n\geq 0
, let
\Xi_{n}
denote the set of finite order characters
\chi:\Gamma\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\times}_{p}
of conductor
a(\chi)=n
. Put
\Xi=\cup_{n}\Xi_{n}
.

ii)

Let
\Xi_{\rm dR}
denote the subset of
{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\times}
-valued characters of
\Gamma
which are de Rham.
Define a partition of
\Xi_{\rm dR}
by

\Xi_{\rm dR}=\Xi_{\phi,\rm dR}^{+}\cup\Xi_{\phi,\rm dR}^{-},\qquad{\Xi}^{\pm}_{\phi,\rm dR}=\{\chi\in\Xi_{\rm dR}\;|\;\ \hat{\varepsilon}({\rm{\mathrm{Ind}}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi\chi^{-1})=\pm 1\}.

We emphasize that the definition of the partition
\Xi_{\phi,\rm dR}^{\pm}
is based on completed epsilon constants of self-dual induced representations, which do not depend on choices of additive character and embedding in view of self-duality, as opposed to
conjugate symplectic self-dual characters.

A primary Iwasawa-theoretic object is the anticyclotomic deformation

{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}=T_{\phi}^{\otimes-1}(1)\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{L}}\Lambda^{\iota},

where
\Lambda^{\iota}
is the
\Lambda
-module free of rank one equipped with

G_{K}
action defined by
ga=[g]^{-1}a
for
g\in G_{K}
and
a\in\Lambda
, and

[g]\in\Gamma(\subseteq\Lambda^{\times})
denotes the image of
g\in G_{K}
under the natural surjection

G_{K}\to\Gamma.

Put

\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}=\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(T_{\phi}^{\otimes-1}(1)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}\Lambda^{\iota}).

Lemma G.22
.

i)

The
\Lambda
-module
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}
is symplectic self-dual of rank two.

ii)

Suppose that
p>3
or
K={\mathbb{Q}}_{3}(\sqrt{3})
if
p=3
.
Then
\tilde{{\mathbb{T}}}_{\phi}
is generic, i.e.
H^{0}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}\otimes_{\Lambda}(\Lambda/\mathfrak{m}_{\Lambda}))=0
.

Proof.

Note that
\Lambda^{\iota}|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}
is the trivial representation
by Lemma
G.12
ii), and so

\displaystyle\det\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}

\displaystyle=\omega_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\cdot(\phi^{-1}\chi_{\rm{cyc}}|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}\otimes\Lambda^{\iota}|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}})=\chi_{\rm{cyc}}

by the determinant formula for induced representations (note that

\chi_{\rm{cyc}}|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}=\chi_{\rm{cyc}}^{2}
),
yielding part i).

As for part ii), first note that
H^{0}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}\otimes(\Lambda/\mathfrak{m}_{\Lambda}))
is isomorphic to

H^{0}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(T_{\phi}^{\otimes-1}(1)\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{L}}{\mathcal{O}}_{L}/{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{L}}))\cong H^{0}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(T_{\phi}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{L}}{\mathcal{O}}_{L}/{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{L}}))\cong H^{0}(K,\overline{T}_{\phi})

for
\overline{T}_{\phi}:=T_{\phi}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{L}}{\mathcal{O}}_{L}/{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{L}}
,
where the second isomorphism is due to the self-duality of
\phi
and the definition of
\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
.
We claim that
H^{0}(K,\overline{T}_{\phi})=0
, i.e.
G_{K}
acts non-trivially on
\overline{T}_{\phi}
.
Indeed,
if
K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
is unramified,
then

p\in K^{\times}
acts on
\overline{T}_{\phi}

as multiplication by
-1
.
As for the ramified case, first suppose that
p>3
.
Then
\overline{\phi}|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}}
is non-trivial.
If
p=3
and
K={\mathbb{Q}}_{3}(\sqrt{3})
, then
\omega_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{3}}(3)=-1
. In turn
\overline{\phi}(\delta)
is a primitive fourth root of unity for
\delta\in K^{\times}
as in §
G.2.3
and so
\delta

acts non-trivially on
\overline{T}_{\phi}
.

∎

Remark G.23
.

The mod
p
reduction of
\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\tilde{{\mathbb{T}}}_{\phi}
is irreducible
if and only if
K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
is
unramified.

A fundamental invariant of
{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}
is the Galois cohomology
H^{1}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi})
. We introduce the following submodules.

Definition G.24
.

For a subset
S
of de Rham specializations of
{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}
,
define

H^{1}_{S,\pm}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi})=\{v\in H^{1}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi})\,|\,s(v)\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi,s})\;\text{for any $s\in S$
with $\hat{\varepsilon}(\tilde{{\mathbb{T}}}_{s})=\mp 1$}\},

where
\tilde{{\mathbb{T}}}_{s}
denotes the specialization of
{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}
at
s
. Similarly, define

H^{1}_{S,\pm}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})=\{v\in H^{1}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\,|\,s(v)\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi,s}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\;\text{for any $s\in S$
with $\hat{\varepsilon}(\tilde{{\mathbb{T}}}_{s})=\mp 1$}\}.

We emphasize that the above definition is based on completed epsilon constants of induced representations.
One seeks to study the
\Lambda
-module structure of the dialectic modules
H^{1}_{S,\pm}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi})
and their relation with
the ambient module

H^{1}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi})
. This is the content of
the next subsection.

G.3.2.
A general Rubin-type conjecture

Theorem G.25
.

Let
p
be an odd prime and
K
a quadratic extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.
Let
\phi
be a de Rham conjugate symplectic self-dual character of
G_{K}

valued in a
p
-adic local field
L
. Put
T_{\phi}={\mathcal{O}}_{L}(\phi)
and
{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}=T_{\phi}^{\otimes-1}(1)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}\Lambda^{\iota}
for
\Lambda
the anticyclotomic Iwasawa algebra over
K
with
{\mathcal{O}}_{L}
-coefficients. Let
S
be a subset of de Rham specializations of
{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}
such that
S\cap\Xi_{\phi,\rm dR}^{+}
and
S\cap\Xi_{\phi,\rm dR}^{-}
are infinite. If
p=3
, suppose that either
K={\mathbb{Q}}_{3}(\sqrt{3})
or
K={\mathbb{Q}}_{3}(\sqrt{-3})
and
\phi\equiv\hskip-10.0pt/\ 1\ \mathrm{mod}\ {{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{L}}}
.

i)

The
\Lambda
-modules
H^{1}_{S,\pm}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi})
are free of rank one and independent of the choice of
S
.

ii)

The
\Lambda
-submodules
H^{1}_{\pm}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}):=H^{1}_{S,\pm}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi})\subset H^{1}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi})
are Lagrangian.

iii)

As
\Lambda
-modules, we have

H^{1}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi})=H^{1}_{+}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi})\oplus H^{1}_{-}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}).

In the excluded case
that
p=3
,
K={\mathbb{Q}}_{3}(\sqrt{-3})
and
\phi\equiv 1\ \mathrm{mod}\ {{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{L}}}
, the assertions as in parts i)-iii) hold
28
28

28

Recall that
H^{i}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}):=H^{i}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}.

We can show that
H^{1}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi})
is
\Lambda
-torsion free.

for the
\Lambda\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-modules
H^{1}_{\cdot}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
.
In particular as
\Lambda\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-modules we have

H^{1}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})=H^{1}_{+}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\oplus H^{1}_{-}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}).

Proof.

By Shapiro’s lemma, note that

H^{1}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi})=H^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},\tilde{{\mathbb{T}}}_{\phi}).

Hence, in view of Lemma
G.22
, the assertions i)-iii) are consequences of the local sign decomposition as in Theorem
3.1
and Corollary
5.3
. Moreover, we have

H^{1}_{\pm}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi})=H^{1}_{\pm}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},\tilde{{\mathbb{T}}}_{\phi}).

(G.7)

As for part iv) note that

H^{0}(K,T_{s})=0

for all characteristic zero specializations
T_{s}
of
{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}
since
T_{s}|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}
is non-trivial, and so
H^{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})=0
by Lemma
2.14
. Hence, Theorem
3.5
yields the local sign decomposition

H^{1}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})=H^{1}_{+}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\oplus H^{1}_{-}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}).

The signed submodules satisfy the property i) by the proof of Corollary
5.3
.
∎

G.3.3.
A Rubin-type conjecture: over the unramified quadratic extension

In this subsection we express Theorem
G.25
in a form closer to the original Rubin conjecture
[
Ru
]
.

Throughout

K
denotes the unramified quadratic extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.
Let
\delta\in K
be a
p
-adic unit such that
K={\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(\delta)
and
\delta^{2}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
.
Let
\phi
be a
p
-adic conjugate symplectic self-dual de Rham character of
G_{K}
.
By Lemma
G.17
,
it is of the form
\phi=\phi_{\pi}\psi_{\pi}^{k}\eta
where
\eta
is a finite character with
\eta|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}=1
.
For simplicity, we say
\eta
is the finite character associated to
\phi
.

Definition G.26
.

Let
\Xi=\Xi^{+}\cup\Xi^{-}
be a partition of finite order non-trivial characters of the anticyclotomic Galois group
\Gamma
defined by

\Xi^{+}=\cup_{n=1}^{\infty}\;\Xi_{2n},\quad\Xi^{-}=\cup_{n=1}^{\infty}\;\Xi_{2n+1}.

Note that the elements of
\Xi^{\pm}
are wildly ramified characters.

Towards Rubin’s original conjecture
[
Ru
]
, we establish the following.

Theorem G.27
.

Let the setting
29
29

29

Rubin assumed that
p\geq 5
, but we also allow
p=3
(cf.
[
89
]
).

be as in §
1.3.2
.

i)

For the conjugate symplectic self-dual character
\phi=\phi_{\pi}
,
there is a canonical
isomorphism

V_{\infty}^{*}\cong H^{1}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}),

and
the decomposition in
Theorem
G.25
iii) coincides with the one conjectured by Rubin
[
Ru
, Conj. 2.2]
.

ii)

We have

\displaystyle V^{*,\pm}_{\infty}=\{v\in V_{\infty}^{*}\;|\;\ \delta_{\chi}(v)=0\quad\text{for every $\chi\in{\Xi}^{\mp}_{\phi,\rm dR}$}\}.

Proof.

For the canonical isomorphism
V_{\infty}^{*}\cong H^{1}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi})
, we refer to

[
15
, §2.2]
, where the identification of
the map
\delta_{\chi}
as in §
1.3.2
with the dual exponential map is also explained.

For
v\in H^{1}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi})
and
\chi\in\Xi^{\mp}
, we denote by
\chi(v)
the image of
v

under the specialization map

H^{1}(K,\mathbb{T}_{\phi})\to H^{1}(K,\mathbb{T}_{\phi})\otimes_{\Lambda,\chi^{-1}}K_{\chi}\to H^{1}(K,T^{\otimes-1}(1)\otimes{\Lambda}\otimes_{\Lambda,\chi^{-1}}K_{\chi})=H^{1}(K,T_{\phi}^{\otimes-1}(1)(\chi))

induced by the
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}
-homomorphism
\Lambda\to K_{\chi}:=K(\mathrm{Im}(\chi))
sending

[\gamma]\in\Gamma
to
\chi^{-1}(\gamma)
.
Let
\exp_{\chi}^{*}
denotes the composition of the above and the dual exponential map for
V_{\phi}^{\otimes-1}(1)(\chi)
.
We put
\varepsilon_{\chi}:=\hat{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(T_{\phi}^{\otimes-1}(1)(\chi))=\hat{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\phi\chi^{-1}))
.

Then, by
[
15
, (2.9)]
,
we have

\displaystyle V_{\infty}^{*,\pm}

\displaystyle=\{v\in H^{1}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi})\;|\;\exp^{*}_{\chi}v=0\;\text{for all $\chi\in\Xi^{\mp}$}\}

\displaystyle=\{v\in H^{1}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi})\;|\;\chi(v)\in H^{1}_{\rm f}(K,T_{\phi}^{\otimes-1}(1)(\chi))\;\text{for all $\chi\in\Xi^{\mp}$}\}

\displaystyle=\{v\in H^{1}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi})\;|\;\chi(v)\in H^{1}_{-\varepsilon_{\chi}}(K,T_{\phi}^{\otimes-1}(1)(\chi))\;\text{for all $\chi\in\Xi^{\mp}$}\}

\displaystyle=\{v\in H^{1}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi})\;|\;\chi(v)\in H^{1}_{\pm}(K,T_{\phi}^{\otimes-1}(1)(\chi))\;\text{for all $\chi\in\Xi^{\mp}$}\}

\displaystyle=H^{1}_{\pm}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}).

Here, the second last equality relies on Proposition
G.4
and
the last on Corollary
5.3
. Then the assertion follows from
Theorem
G.25
.
∎

Remark G.28
.

Part i) has been proved in
[
BKO21
]
by a different method, which led to a new proof
[
18
]
of Kato’s local epsilon conjecture in this case.
Moreover, part ii) has been proved except for finitely many
\chi

in
[
18
, §5]
by using
elliptic units and Kato’s explicit reciprocity law.

Now we explicate Theorem
G.25
for general conjugate symplectic self-dual characters.

Theorem G.29
.

Let
p
be an odd prime and
K
the unramified quadratic extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.
Let
\phi
be a de Rham conjugate symplectic self-dual character of
G_{K}
with Hodge–Tate weights
(k+1,-k)
for
k\geq 0
and of finite character
\eta
.
Put
T_{\phi}={\mathcal{O}}_{K}(\phi)

and
{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}=T_{\phi}^{\otimes-1}(1)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\Lambda^{\iota}
for
\Lambda
the anticyclotomic Iwasawa algebra over
K
with
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}
-coefficients.

i)

The
\Lambda
-modules
H^{1}_{\pm}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi})
as in Theorem
G.25
are given by

H^{1}_{\pm}(K,\mathbb{T}_{\phi})=\{x\in H^{1}(K,\mathbb{T}_{\phi})\;|\;\exp^{*}_{\chi}(x)=0\;\text{for almost all $\chi\in S^{\mp}$}\}

where

S^{\mp}=\begin{cases}\Xi^{\mp}\quad(k:\text{even}),\\
\Xi^{\pm}\quad(k:\text{odd})\end{cases}\text{if $\eta(\delta)=1$},\quad S^{\mp}=\begin{cases}\Xi^{\pm}\quad(k:\text{even}),\\
\Xi^{\mp}\quad(k:\text{odd})\end{cases}\text{if $\eta(\delta)=-1$}.

Moreover, if
\eta
is at most tamely ramified, this remains true for all characters
\chi\in S^{\mp}
and

\Xi^{+}
can be replaced with
\Xi^{+}\cup\{\rm{1}\}
.

ii)

The
\Lambda
-submodules
H^{1}_{\pm}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi})\subset H^{1}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi})
are Lagrangian.

iii)

As
\Lambda
-modules, we have

H^{1}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi})=H^{1}_{+}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi})\oplus H^{1}_{-}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi}).

Proof.

By Propositions
G.18
,
G.4
and
2.5
, the corresponding
\varepsilon
-constant is given by

\varepsilon(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi\chi)=\varepsilon(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi_{K}\eta\chi)={\eta(\delta)}(-1)^{a(\eta\chi)}.

Then the completed
\varepsilon
-constant equals
{\eta(\delta)}(-1)^{a(\eta\chi)+k}
.
∎

Corollary G.30
.

For any crystalline conjugate symplectic self-dual character
\phi
of
G_{K}
with Hodge–Tate weights
(k+1,-k)
and
k\geq 1
, for example

\phi=\phi_{\pi}\psi_{\pi}^{k}
as in Lemma
G.17
, the decomposition as in Theorem
G.29
ii)
coincides with the one conjectured by Büyükboduk
[
Kazim
]
,
resolving Conjectures 2.5 and 2.13 of loc. cit.

Remark G.31
.

Theorem
G.29
and Corollary
G.30
have applications
to CM Iwasawa theory at inert primes
p
.
For example, it leads to an integral
p
-adic
L
-function and a main conjecture, generalizing our results in the good reduction case
[
BKO21
]
to the non-semistable reduction case, and it also
resolves the signed Iwasawa main conjecture in
[
Kazim
, Thm. B]
.

In the following we consider higher weight specializations of the signed submodules.

Let
\omega_{2}
be the character of
G_{K}
such that
\omega_{2}(-p)=1
and

\omega_{2}|_{\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}}
is the Teichmüller character.
The anticyclotomic character
\psi_{\pi}=\phi_{\pi}/\phi_{\pi}^{*}
has trivial central character and

\psi_{\pi}|_{K^{\times}_{\mathrm{tor}}}=\omega_{2}^{1-p}
.
Hence,
\psi_{\Gamma}:=\psi_{\pi}\omega_{2}^{p-1}
factors through the anticyclotomic Galois group
\Gamma
by Lemma
G.14
.

Corollary G.32
.

Let
\eta
be a finite character of
G_{K}
such that
\eta|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}=1
.
For an integer
l\geq 0
, let
\epsilon_{l}
denote the sign of

(-1)^{l}\left(\frac{-1}{p}\right)^{l}
.
Then we have

H^{1}_{\pm}(K,\mathbb{T}_{\phi_{\pi}\eta})=\{x\in H^{1}(K,\mathbb{T}_{\phi_{\pi}\eta})\;|\;\exp^{*}_{\psi_{\Gamma}^{l}\chi}(x)=0\;\text{for $\chi\in S^{\mp\epsilon_{l}}$ with $a(\chi)>a(\eta)$}\},

where
\exp^{*}_{\psi_{\Gamma}^{l}\chi}
is the composition of

\begin{split}H^{1}(K,\mathbb{T}_{\phi_{\pi}\eta})&=\varprojlim_{m}H^{1}(K_{m},T_{\phi_{\pi}\eta}^{\otimes-1}(1))\cong\varprojlim_{m}H^{1}(K_{m},T_{\phi_{\pi}\eta}^{\otimes-1}(1))\otimes\psi_{\Gamma}^{-l}=\varprojlim_{m}H^{1}(K_{m},T_{\phi_{\pi}\eta\psi_{\Gamma}^{l}}^{\otimes-1}(1))\\
&\rightarrow H^{1}(K,T_{\phi_{\pi}\eta\psi_{\Gamma}^{l}}^{\otimes-1}(1)(\chi))\end{split}

and the dual exponential map.
In particular, we have

H^{1}_{\pm}(K,\mathbb{T}_{\phi_{\pi}\eta})\otimes\psi_{\Gamma}^{-l}=H^{1}_{\pm\epsilon_{l}}(K,\mathbb{T}_{\phi_{\pi}\psi_{\Gamma}^{l}\eta}).

Proof.

Put
\xi:=\omega_{2}^{(p-1)l}
for simplicity.
Then the Weil–Deligne character associated with

\phi_{\pi}\psi_{\Gamma}^{l}\eta\chi
is
\phi_{K}\xi\eta\chi
for any
\chi\in\Xi
.

So we have

\hat{\varepsilon}({\rm Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi_{\pi}\psi_{\Gamma}^{l}\eta\chi)=(-1)^{l}\varepsilon({\rm Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi_{K}\xi\eta\chi)=(-1)^{l}\eta\xi\chi(\delta)(-1)^{a(\xi\eta\chi)}=(-1)^{l}\left(\frac{-1}{p}\right)^{l}(-1)^{a(\xi\eta\chi)}\eta\chi(\delta)

by Proposition
G.4
.
If
a(\chi)>a(\xi\eta)
, then we in turn have

\hat{\varepsilon}({\rm Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi_{\pi}\psi_{\Gamma}^{l}\chi)=\epsilon_{l}\cdot\hat{\varepsilon}({\rm Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi_{\pi}\chi).

Hence, the assertion follows from
Theorem
G.25
i).
∎

G.3.4.
A Rubin-type conjecture: over ramified quadratic extensions

The main result of this subsection is
Theorem
G.36
.

Throughout
K
denotes a ramified quadratic extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.
Let
\delta\in K
be a uniformizer such that
K={\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(\delta)
and
\delta^{2}\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.
For a more concrete formulation of the Rubin-type conjecture,
let
\phi_{1}
be a conjugate symplectic self-dual
p
-adic de Rham character of
G_{K}

with Hodge–Tate weights
(k+1,-k)
so that
k\geq 0
. We assume that
the Weil–Deligne character associated to
\phi_{1}
has
conductor
1
.

For a finite order character
\chi:\Gamma\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}
, put

\varepsilon_{\phi_{1}}(\chi):=\begin{cases}\phi_{1}(\delta)\delta^{-1}\sum_{a\in{\mathbb{F}}_{p}^{\times}}\left(\frac{a}{p}\right)\chi(1+\delta^{a(\chi)-1})^{a}\qquad&(\chi\not=1)\\
\;1\qquad&(\chi=1).\end{cases}

Note that
\varepsilon_{\phi_{1}}(\chi)\in\{\pm 1\}
and it is independent of the choice of
\delta
.

Lemma G.33
.

Let
\phi_{1}
be a conjugate symplectic self-dual
p
-adic de Rham character of
G_{K}
of conductor one
with Hodge–Tate weights
(k+1,-k)
for
k\geq 0
, and
\chi:\Gamma\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}
a finite order character.

i)

We have

\hat{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi_{1}\chi)=(-1)^{k}\omega_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(-2)\left(\frac{-1}{p}\right)^{\frac{a(\chi)}{2}}\varepsilon_{\phi_{1}}(\chi).

ii)

For
b\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
, we have

\hat{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi_{1}\chi^{b})=\left(\frac{b}{p}\right)\hat{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi_{1}\chi)

Moreover, if
\chi^{p}\not=1
, then

\hat{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi_{1}\chi^{-\delta^{2}})=\hat{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi_{1}\chi).

Proof.

Part i) follows
from Propositions
G.7
and
G.20
since
\chi(\delta)=1
, and ii) from
Corollary
G.8
.
Note that
\phi_{1}(\delta)\delta^{-1}
is the value at
\delta
of
the Weil–Deligne character of conductor
1
associated to
\phi_{1}
, and
that
a(\chi)
is even by Lemma
G.5
iii).
∎

Definition G.34
.

Let
\Xi_{n}=\Xi^{+}_{\phi_{1},n}\cup\Xi^{-}_{\phi_{1},n}

be a partition of finite order anticyclotomic characters of
\Gamma
of (additive) conductor
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
defined by

\Xi^{\pm}_{\phi_{1},n}=\{\chi\in\Xi_{n}\;|\>\hat{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi_{1}\chi^{-1})=\pm 1\}.

Put
\Xi^{\pm}_{\phi_{1}}=\cup_{n=0}^{\infty}\Xi^{\pm}_{\phi_{1},n}
.

Note that
\Xi_{n}=\emptyset
for
n
odd by Lemma
G.5
iii).

Lemma G.35
.

i)

For
n\geq 2
, we have

\#\Xi^{+}_{\phi_{1},n}=\#\Xi^{-}_{\phi_{1},n}.

ii)

The partition
\Xi_{n}=\Xi^{+}_{\phi_{1},n}\cup\Xi^{-}_{\phi_{1},n}
is canonical. Moreover, its labeling depends on the choice of
\phi_{1}
and it is determined by the labeling of
\Xi_{2}
.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
G.33
ii).
In fact, pick an element
u\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
for which

\left(\frac{u}{p}\right)=-1
. Then, note that

\chi\in\Xi^{+}_{\phi_{1},n}
if and only if
\chi^{u}\in\Xi^{-}_{\phi_{1},n}
, and

\chi\in\Xi^{\pm}_{\phi_{1},n+2}
if and only if
\chi^{-\delta^{2}}\in\Xi^{\pm}_{\phi_{1},n}
.

∎

Theorem G.36
.

Let
p
be an odd prime and
K
a ramified quadratic extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.
Let
\phi_{1}
be a de Rham conjugate symplectic self-dual character of
K^{\times}
with Hodge–Tate weights
(k+1,-k)
for
k\geq 0
and
valued in a
p
-adic local field
L
.
Suppose that the Weil–Deligne character associated to
\phi_{1}
has conductor
1
.
Put
T_{\phi_{1}}={\mathcal{O}}_{L}(\phi_{1})

and
{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi_{1}}=T_{\phi_{1}}^{\otimes-1}(1)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}\Lambda^{\iota}
for
\Lambda
the anticyclotomic Iwasawa algebra over
K
with
{\mathcal{O}}_{L}
-coefficients.
If
p=3
, suppose that either
K={\mathbb{Q}}_{3}(\sqrt{3})
, or
K={\mathbb{Q}}_{3}(\sqrt{-3})
and
\phi_{1}\equiv\hskip-10.0pt/\ 1\ \mathrm{mod}\ {{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{L}}}
.

i)

The
\Lambda
-modules
H^{1}_{\pm}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi_{1}})
as in Theorem
G.25
are given by

H^{1}_{\pm}(K,\mathbb{T}_{\phi_{1}})=\{x\in H^{1}(K,\mathbb{T}_{\phi_{1}})\;|\;\exp^{*}_{\chi}(x)=0\;\text{for all $\chi\in\Xi_{\phi_{1},n}^{\mp}$}\}.

ii)

The
\Lambda
-submodules
H^{1}_{\pm}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi_{1}})\subset H^{1}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi_{1}})
are Lagrangian.

iii)

As
\Lambda
-modules, we have

H^{1}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi_{1}})=H^{1}_{+}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi_{1}})\oplus H^{1}_{-}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi_{1}}).

In the excluded case that
p=3
,
K={\mathbb{Q}}_{3}(\sqrt{-3})
and
\phi_{1}\equiv 1\ \mathrm{mod}\ {{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{L}}}
, the assertions as in parts i)-iii) hold for the
\Lambda\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-modules
H^{1}_{\cdot}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi_{1}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
.

Proof.

By Theorem
G.25
i),
we have

H^{1}_{\pm}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\phi_{1}})=\{x\in H^{1}(K,\mathbb{T}_{\phi_{1}})\;|\;\exp^{*}_{\chi}(x)=0\quad\text{for all $\chi\in\Xi^{\mp}_{\phi_{1}}$}\}.

Hence, Theorem
G.25
concludes the proof.
∎

Note that the anticyclotomic character
\psi_{1}=\phi_{1}/\phi_{1}^{*}
has trivial central character and it factors through the anticyclotomic Galois group
\Gamma
by Lemma
G.14
.
We have the following description of signed submodules under higher weight specializations.

Corollary G.37
.

For an integer
l\geq 0
, let
\epsilon_{l}
denote the sign of

(-1)^{l}\left(\frac{-1}{p}\right)^{l}.

Then we have

\displaystyle H^{1}_{\pm}(K,\mathbb{T}_{\phi_{1}})=\{x\in H^{1}(K,\mathbb{T}_{\phi_{1}})\;|\;\exp^{*}_{\psi_{1}^{l}\chi}(x)=0\;\text{for all $\chi\in\Xi^{\mp\epsilon_{l}}_{\phi_{1}}$}\},

where
\exp^{*}_{\psi_{1}^{l}\chi}
is defined in the same way as in
Corollary
G.32
.
In particular,

\displaystyle H^{1}_{\pm}(K,\mathbb{T}_{\phi_{1}})\otimes\psi_{1}^{-l}

\displaystyle=H^{1}_{\pm\epsilon_{l}}(K,\mathbb{T}_{\phi_{1}\psi_{1}^{l}}).

Proof.

The Weil–Deligne characters associated to
\phi_{1}\psi_{1}^{l}
is and
\phi_{1}
differ by
\epsilon_{l}
by Proposition
G.20
. So we have

\hat{\varepsilon}({\rm Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi_{1}\psi_{1}^{l}\chi)=(-1)^{l}\left(\frac{-1}{p}\right)^{l}\hat{\varepsilon}({\rm Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\phi_{1}\chi).

Therefore,
Theorem
G.25
i) concludes the proof.
∎

Appendix H
p
-adic
L
-functions for CM elliptic curves at additive primes

In this section we consider an elliptic curve defined over
{\mathbb{Q}}
with complex multiplication by
an order of an imaginary quadratic field
\mathcal{K}
. For primes
p
ramified in
{\mathcal{K}}
, we construct a bounded
p
-adic
L
-function for
the deformation of the
p
-adic Tate module of
E

arising from the anticyclotomic
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-extension
{\mathcal{K}}_{\infty}^{\rm ac}
of
{\mathcal{K}}
, which is integral except the case
p=3
and

{\mathcal{K}}={\mathbb{Q}}(\sqrt{-3})
(see Theorem
H.15
).
We also establish a formula for the variation of Mordell–Weil rank of
E
along the subextensions of
{\mathcal{K}}_{\infty}^{\rm ac}/{\mathcal{K}}
(see Theorem
H.4
).

In the sequel
[
13
]
we develop anticyclotomic CM Iwasawa theory at primes
p
ramified in the CM field
more generally, including CM abelian varieties over
{\mathbb{Q}}
and CM elliptic curves defined over abelian extensions of
{\mathcal{K}}
.
To highlight the phenomena, we only consider CM curves over
{\mathbb{Q}}
in this section.

H.1.
Set-up

H.1.1.

Let
A
be a CM elliptic curve defined over
{\mathbb{Q}}
and
{\mathcal{K}}
the CM field.
Let
\omega_{{\mathcal{K}}/{\mathbb{Q}}}
denote the quadratic character associated to the extension
{\mathcal{K}}/{\mathbb{Q}}
.

Note that the class number of
\mathcal{K}
equals
1
and
\mathcal{K}={\mathbb{Q}}(\sqrt{-q})

for

q\in\{1,2,3,7,11,19,43,67,163\}.

We suppose that
q\geq 3
and put
p=q
.
Then
p
is the unique ramified prime in
\mathcal{K}
, and we write
p{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{K}}={\mathfrak{p}}^{2}
.
Put
K:=\mathcal{K}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}={\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(\sqrt{-p})
.

Fix embeddings
\iota_{\infty}:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}
and
\iota_{p}:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
. Let

[\;]:\mathcal{K}\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}\mathrm{End}(A)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbb{Q}}

be the isomorphism so that

[a]^{*}\omega_{A}=a\omega_{A}
for a Néron differential
\omega_{A}
of
A
and any
a\in\mathcal{K}
.
Let
\boldsymbol{\phi}
be the global
p
-adic Hecke character associated to
A
.
Then the
p
-adic Tate module
T_{p}A
naturally has a structure of
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}
-module free of rank one on which
G_{{\mathcal{K}}}
acts by
\boldsymbol{\phi}
,
and denote by
T_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}
the
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}[G_{{\mathcal{K}}}]
-module.
Put
V_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}=T_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}K
.

Lemma H.1
.

We have a canonical isomorphism

V_{p}A\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}K\cong\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathcal{K}/{\mathbb{Q}}}\,V_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}

of
K[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}]
-modules.

Proof.

There exists a
K[G_{\mathcal{K}}]
-equivariant canonical projection

V_{p}A\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}K\cong V_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}\oplus V_{\boldsymbol{\phi}^{c}}\rightarrow V_{\boldsymbol{\phi}},

where
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{c}:={\boldsymbol{\phi}}\circ c
for
c\in{\mathrm{Gal}}({\mathcal{K}}/{\mathbb{Q}})
the non-trivial element.
Note that
V_{p}A\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}K
has
structure of a
K[G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}]
-representation,
extending the
K[G_{\mathcal{K}}]
-action.
Therefore, by the adjoint property of induced representations and
the irreducibility of
V_{p}A\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}K
, the assertion follows.
∎

For
\cdot\in\{\emptyset,\otimes^{-1}\}
,
put
V_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\cdot}=T_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\cdot}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}K
.

H.1.2.

Let
{\mathcal{K}}_{\infty}^{\rm ac}
be the anticyclotomic
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-extension of
{\mathcal{K}}
and
{\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac}
its subextension of degree
p^{n}
over
{\mathcal{K}}
for an integer
n\geq 0
. Put
\Lambda:=\mathcal{O}_{K}[\![\mathrm{Gal}(\mathcal{K}^{\rm ac}_{\infty}/\mathcal{K})]\!]
.

We have the following variation of global epsilon constants along the anticyclotomic tower.

Proposition H.2
.

Let
A
be a CM elliptic curve defined over
{\mathbb{Q}}
and
{\mathcal{K}}
the CM field.
Let
p
be an odd prime ramified in
{\mathcal{K}}
,
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}
the associated
p
-adic character of
G_{\mathcal{K}}
and
K
the localization of
{\mathcal{K}}
at the prime above
p
.
Let
\chi:{\mathrm{Gal}}({\mathcal{K}}_{\infty}^{\rm ac}/{\mathcal{K}})\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\times}
be a finite order character.

i)

We have

\frac{\varepsilon(\boldsymbol{\phi}\chi)}{\varepsilon(\boldsymbol{\phi})}=\frac{\varepsilon_{p}({\rm Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\boldsymbol{\phi}\chi)}{\varepsilon_{p}({\rm Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\boldsymbol{\phi})}.

ii)

For
b\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
,

\varepsilon(\boldsymbol{\phi}\chi^{b})=\left(\frac{b}{p}\right)\varepsilon(\boldsymbol{\phi}\chi).

iii)

If
\chi^{p}\not=1
, then

\varepsilon(\boldsymbol{\phi}\chi^{-\delta^{2}})=\varepsilon(\boldsymbol{\phi}\chi)

for
\delta\in K
a uniformizer
30
30

30

One may choose
\delta
so that
\delta^{2}=-p
.
such that
\delta^{2}\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.

iv)

For an integer
n\geq 1
, let
\Xi_{{\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac}}
denote the set of
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\times}
-valued characters of
{\mathrm{Gal}}({\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac}/{\mathcal{K}})

not factoring through
{\mathrm{Gal}}({\mathcal{K}}_{n-1}^{\rm ac}/{\mathcal{K}})
. Then we have

\#\{\chi\in\Xi_{{\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac}}|\varepsilon(\boldsymbol{\phi}\chi)=+1\}=\#\{\chi\in\Xi_{{\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac}}|\varepsilon(\boldsymbol{\phi}\chi)=-1\}.

Proof.

i) Note that

\frac{\varepsilon(\boldsymbol{\phi}\chi)}{\varepsilon(\boldsymbol{\phi})}=\frac{\varepsilon({\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{K}/{\mathbb{Q}}}\boldsymbol{\phi}\chi)}{\varepsilon({\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{K}/{\mathbb{Q}}}\boldsymbol{\phi})}=\frac{\hat{\varepsilon}_{p}({\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{K}/{\mathbb{Q}}}\boldsymbol{\phi}\chi)}{\hat{\varepsilon}_{p}({\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{K}/{\mathbb{Q}}}\boldsymbol{\phi})}.\prod_{l\not=p}\frac{\varepsilon_{l}({\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{K}/{\mathbb{Q}}}\boldsymbol{\phi}\chi)}{\varepsilon_{l}({\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{K}/{\mathbb{Q}}}\boldsymbol{\phi})}.

Hence, for a place
v|l
of
K
, it suffices to show that
the local epsilon constants

\varepsilon_{v}({\boldsymbol{\phi}}_{v}\chi,\psi_{v},dx_{v})
and
\varepsilon_{v}({\boldsymbol{\phi}}_{v},\psi_{v},dx_{v})

are equal up to a
p
-power root of unity, where
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}_{v}
is the Weil–Deligne character associated to

{\boldsymbol{\phi}}|_{G_{K_{v}}}
,
\psi_{v}
is a non-trivial additive character of
K_{v}
,
and
dx_{v}
is a Haar measure on
K_{v}
.
This equality is a direct consequence of (
G.3
) since
\chi
is unramified at
v
.

Parts ii)-iv) follow from Lemma
G.33
iii).
∎

Definition H.3
.

For
n\geq 1
, let
\Xi_{{\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac}}=\Xi_{{\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac},\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{+}\cup\Xi_{{\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac},\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{-}

be a partition of
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\times}
-valued
characters of
{\mathrm{Gal}}({\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac}/{\mathcal{K}})

defined by

\Xi_{{\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac},\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\pm}=\{\chi\in\Xi_{{\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac}}|\varepsilon(\boldsymbol{\phi}\chi^{-1})=\pm 1\}.

For
\cdot\in\{\emptyset,+,-\}
,
put
\Xi_{{\mathcal{K}}_{\infty}^{\rm ac},\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\cdot}=\cup_{n}\Xi_{{\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac},\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\cdot}
.

If
\chi\in\Xi_{{\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac},{\boldsymbol{\phi}}}^{-}
, then
\varepsilon({\boldsymbol{\phi}}\chi^{-1})=-1
and so

L(\boldsymbol{\phi}\chi^{-1},1)=0

for
L(\boldsymbol{\phi}\chi,s)
the associated Hecke
L
-function with center at the point
s=1
.
In view of this vanishing one may seek
p
-adic interpolation of a normalization of algebraic part of the Hecke
L
-values
L({\boldsymbol{\phi}}\chi^{-1},1)
for
\chi\in\Xi_{{\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac},{\boldsymbol{\phi}}}^{+}
. A main result of this section is the existence of such a bounded
p
-adic
L
-function (see Theorem
H.15
).

H.2.
Variation of Mordell–Weil ranks

This subsection establishes a formula for the Mordell–Weil rank of the CM elliptic curve
A
along subextensions of the anticyclotomic
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-extension
{\mathcal{K}}_{\infty}^{\rm ac}/{\mathcal{K}}
.

The main result of this subsection is the following.

Theorem H.4
.

Let
A
be a CM elliptic curve defined over
{\mathbb{Q}}
and
{\mathcal{K}}
the CM field.
Let
p
be an odd prime ramified in
{\mathcal{K}}
and
{\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac}
the subextension of the anticyclotomic
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-extension
{\mathcal{K}}_{\infty}^{\rm ac}/{\mathcal{K}}
of degree
p^{n}
over
{\mathcal{K}}
.
Then there exists a constant
c\in{\mathbb{Z}}
such that for any sufficiently large integer
n
, we have

{\mathrm{rank}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\,A({\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac})=p^{n}+c.

We begin with some preliminaries.

For a finite order character
\chi
of
{\mathrm{Gal}}({\mathcal{K}}_{\infty}^{\rm ac}/{\mathcal{K}})
,
let
f_{\chi}\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(D_{K}N_{{\mathcal{K}}/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}({\boldsymbol{\phi}}\chi)))
denote the theta series associated to the conjugate symplectic self-dual
31
31

31

the natural global analogue of Definition
G.9
Hecke character
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}\chi
over
{\mathcal{K}}
, where
\mathrm{cond^{r}}(\cdot)
denotes the conductor.
In particular, we have

L(f_{\chi},s)=L({\boldsymbol{\phi}}\chi,s).

Denote by
A_{\chi}
the CM abelian variety defined over
{\mathbb{Q}}
associated to
f_{\chi}
. Note that

L(A_{\chi}/{\mathbb{Q}},s)=\prod_{\sigma:F_{\chi}\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}}L(f_{\chi}^{\sigma},s)

where
F_{\chi}
is the Hecke field of
f_{\chi}
.
Suppose that the order of
\chi
is
p^{n}
.
Then
F_{\chi}
is the maximal totally real field in
{\mathbb{Q}}(\zeta_{p^{n}})
.
Note that
\mathcal{K}={\mathbb{Q}}(\sqrt{-p})\subseteq{\mathbb{Q}}(\zeta_{p^{n}})
, and so

{\mathrm{End}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{0}(A_{\chi})\cong F_{\chi}
and

\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{K}}^{0}(A_{\chi})\cong\mathcal{K}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}F_{\chi}\cong{\mathbb{Q}}(\zeta_{p^{n}})

where
\mathrm{End}^{0}:=\mathrm{End}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
.
As
G_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
-representations, we have

V_{p}A_{\chi}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}\cong\prod_{\sigma}\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathcal{K}/{\mathbb{Q}}}\;\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}({\boldsymbol{\phi}}\chi^{\sigma})=\prod_{a\in({\mathbb{Z}}/p^{n}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times},\left(\frac{a}{p}\right)=1}\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathcal{K}/{\mathbb{Q}}}\;\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}({\boldsymbol{\phi}}\chi^{a})

(H.1)

where
\chi^{\sigma}
runs through characters of
{\mathrm{Gal}}({\mathbb{Q}}(\zeta_{p^{n}})/{\mathcal{K}})\cong{\mathrm{Gal}}(F_{\chi}/{\mathbb{Q}})
.

We have the following fundamental result towards the Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer conjecture due to Gross–Zagier
[
40
]
and Kolyvagin (cf.
[
15
, Prop. 3.5]
).

Proposition H.5
.

Suppose that
\mathrm{ord}_{s=1}L({\boldsymbol{\phi}}\chi,s)\in\{0,1\}
.
Then
\mathrm{dim}_{F_{\chi}}A_{\chi}({\mathbb{Q}})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbb{Q}}=\mathrm{ord}_{s=1}L({\boldsymbol{\phi}}\chi,s)
.

Proof of Theorem
H.4
.

Let
B_{n}
denote
the Weil restriction
{\mathrm{Res}}_{{\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac}/{\mathcal{K}}}(A_{/{\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac}})
of
A
over
{\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac}
.
By
[
31
, Thm. 3]
, we have

\mathrm{End}^{0}(B_{n})=\mathrm{End}^{0}(A)[{\mathrm{Gal}}({\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac}/{\mathcal{K}})]=\mathcal{K}[{\mathrm{Gal}}({\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac}/{\mathcal{K}})].

Here, the action of
{\mathrm{Gal}}({\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac}/{\mathcal{K}})
on
B_{n}(\mathcal{K})
by the above equality is compatible with
the Galois action of
A({\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac})
arising from the canonical identification
B_{n}(\mathcal{K})=A({\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac})
.

Let
\Phi_{p^{n}}(X)
be the
p^{n}
-th cyclotomic polynomial and
let
\Phi_{p^{n}}(X)=\Phi_{p^{n}}^{+}(X)\Phi_{p^{n}}^{-}(X)
be the decomposition into monic polynomials in
\mathcal{K}[X]
.
Fix a generator
\gamma
of
{\mathrm{Gal}}({\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac}/{\mathcal{K}})
and put

R_{n}^{\cdot}:=\mathcal{K}[{\mathrm{Gal}}({\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac}/{\mathcal{K}})]/(\Phi_{p^{n}}^{\cdot}(\gamma))

for
\cdot\in\{\emptyset,\pm\}
.
Then a character

\chi:{\mathrm{Gal}}({\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac}/{\mathcal{K}})\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\times}
of order
p^{n}

determines a
specialization

R_{n}=R_{n}^{+}\times R_{n}^{-}\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}.

According to Proposition
H.2
ii), we can give the sign labeling of
\Phi_{n}^{\pm}(X)
so that

\chi
factors through
R_{n}^{+}
if and only if
\varepsilon({\boldsymbol{\phi}}\chi)=+1
.

We have a decomposition of
\mathcal{K}
-algebras

\mathrm{End}^{0}(B_{n})\cong R_{n}^{+}\times R_{n}^{-}\times R^{\prime},

and hence the isogeny decomposition

B_{n}\sim A_{n}^{+}\times A_{n}^{-}\times A^{\prime}

for abelian varieties over
\mathcal{K}
. By construction, note that

A({\mathcal{K}}_{n-1}^{\rm ac})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbb{Q}}=A^{\prime}(\mathcal{K})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbb{Q}}
and

(A({\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac})/A({\mathcal{K}}_{n-1}^{\rm ac}))\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbb{Q}}\cong\left(A_{n}^{+}({\mathcal{K}})\oplus A_{n}^{-}({\mathcal{K}})\right)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbb{Q}}.

Note also that

V_{p}A_{n}^{\pm}\otimes_{{K}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}\cong V_{p}A\otimes_{{K}}\hat{R}_{n}^{\pm}\otimes_{{K}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}\cong\prod_{\chi:R_{n}^{\pm}\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}({\boldsymbol{\phi}}\chi)

as
G_{\mathcal{K}}
-representations where
\hat{R}_{n}^{\pm}:={R}_{n}^{\pm}\otimes_{\mathcal{K}}K
.
Hence, by (
H.1
), we have

V_{p}A_{n}^{\pm}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}\cong V_{p}A_{\chi}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}|_{G_{\mathcal{K}}}

for any
\chi
factoring through
R_{n}^{\pm}
.
Then we have

A_{n}^{\pm}\sim A_{\chi}
over
\mathcal{K}
by Faltings.
In particular, we may assume that
A_{n}^{\pm}
is of the form
A_{\chi}
and defined over
{\mathbb{Q}}
.

Put
V=A^{\pm}_{n}(\mathcal{K})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbb{Q}}
.
For the non-trivial element
c\in\mathrm{Gal}(\mathcal{K}/{\mathbb{Q}})
, consider the eigen decomposition

V=V^{c=1}\oplus V^{c=-1}.

Then,
V^{c=1}=A^{\pm}_{n}({\mathbb{Q}})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbb{Q}}
, and the multiplication by a purely totally imaginary element
in
\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{K}}^{0}(A_{n}^{\pm})\cong{\mathbb{Q}}(\zeta_{p^{n}})
gives an isomorphism

V^{c=1}\cong V^{c=-1}
. Hence,

\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}(A^{\pm}_{n}(\mathcal{K})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbb{Q}})=2\dim_{{\mathbb{Q}}}(A^{\pm}_{n}({\mathbb{Q}})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbb{Q}}).

By Rohrlich
[
78
]
,
for sufficiently large
n
,
we have

{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L({\boldsymbol{\phi}}\chi,s)=\frac{1-\varepsilon({\boldsymbol{\phi}}\chi)}{2}.

Hence, by Proposition
H.5
,
we have

{\mathrm{rank}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}A_{n}^{+}({\mathbb{Q}})=0,\quad{\mathrm{rank}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}A_{n}^{-}({\mathbb{Q}})=(p^{n}-p^{n-1})/2

for almost all
n
.
Therefore,
{\mathrm{rank}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}A({\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac})-{\mathrm{rank}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}A({\mathcal{K}}_{n-1}^{\rm ac})=p^{n}-p^{n-1}
for any such
n
,
and the assertion follows.
∎

Remark H.6
.

A generalization to CM elliptic curves not necessarily defined over
{\mathbb{Q}}
will appear in
[
13
]
.

H.3.
Elliptic units

This subsection describes preliminaries regarding elliptic units following Kato
[
49
, §15.3]
.

H.3.1.
Set-up

We consider a slightly more general set-up. Let
{\mathcal{K}}
be any imaginary quadratic field and
p
an odd prime.

For a non-zero ideal
{\mathfrak{g}}
of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{K}}
,
let
\mathcal{K}({\mathfrak{g}})
denote the ray
class field
of conductor
{\mathfrak{g}}
. If
{\mathfrak{g}}=c{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{K}}
for some
c\in{\mathbb{Z}}\setminus\{0\},

then let
\mathcal{K}[{\mathfrak{g}}]
denote the ring class field associated to the order
{\mathbb{Z}}+c{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{K}}}
of
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}
.
Put

\mathcal{K}[p^{\infty}{\mathfrak{g}}]=\cup_{m\geq 0}\mathcal{K}[p^{m}{\mathfrak{g}}]
,

\Lambda_{{\mathfrak{g}}}={\mathcal{O}}_{K}[\![{\mathrm{Gal}}(\mathcal{K}[p^{\infty}{\mathfrak{g}}]/\mathcal{K})]\!]

and
Q(\Lambda_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
its total quotient ring.
For a finitely generated
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-module
T
with continuous action of
G_{\mathcal{K}}
and
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
define Iwasawa cohomology groups

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}^{i}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}(T)

\displaystyle=\varprojlim_{n}H^{i}(\mathcal{K}(p^{n}{\mathfrak{g}}),T),

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}^{i}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}(T\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{H}^{i}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}(T)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p},

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}^{i}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\mathrm{ac}}(T)

\displaystyle=\varprojlim_{n}H^{i}(\mathcal{K}[p^{n}{\mathfrak{g}}],T),\quad

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}^{i}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\mathrm{ac}}(T\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{H}^{i}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\mathrm{ac}}(T)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}.

If
{\mathfrak{g}}=(1)
, then we omit
{\mathfrak{g}}
in the notation above.

Let
\mathfrak{f}
be a non-zero ideal of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{K}}

such that the natural map
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{K}}}^{\times}\rightarrow({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{K}}/\mathfrak{f})^{\times}

is injective
32
32

32

If
\mathcal{K}
is neither
{\mathbb{Q}}(i)
nor
{\mathbb{Q}}(\sqrt{-3})
, then
this holds if
\mathfrak{f}\not=(2)
.
.
To define elliptic units, we introduce another CM elliptic curve
E

defined over
K({\mathfrak{f}})
with
an isomorphism
\iota:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}\cong\mathrm{End}(E)
such that the composite

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}\xrightarrow{\iota}\mathrm{End}(E)\rightarrow\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{Lie}(E))=\mathcal{K}^{\prime}

coincides with the natural inclusion.
We may assume that
there exists a generator
\alpha
of
E[{\mathfrak{f}}]
and an isomorphism
E\cong{\mathbb{C}}/{\mathfrak{f}}
over
{\mathbb{C}}

sending
\alpha
to
1\bmod{\mathfrak{f}}
.
Such a CM pair
(E,\alpha)
(also with
\iota
) exists uniquely up to a canonical isomorphism (cf.
[
49
, (15.3.1)]
).
Let
\mathfrak{a}
be an ideal of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}
coprime to
6p\mathfrak{f}
.

Let

{}_{\mathfrak{a}}z_{\mathfrak{f}}:={}_{\mathfrak{a}}\theta_{E}(\alpha)^{-1}\in\mathcal{K}(\mathfrak{f})^{\times}

(H.2)

be the standard elliptic unit of modulus
\mathfrak{f}

as in
[
49
, §15.4]
.
For an ideal
{\mathfrak{g}}\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{K}}
, let

\!{}_{{\mathfrak{a}}}z_{p^{n}{\mathfrak{g}}}\in H^{1}(\mathcal{K}(p^{n}{\mathfrak{g}}),{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}(1))

also denote the image of

\!{}_{{\mathfrak{a}}}z_{p^{n}{\mathfrak{g}}}
as in (
H.2
)
under the Kummer map. (Here, we assume
p^{n}{\mathfrak{g}}
satisfies the condition on
{\mathfrak{f}}
above.)
Then
\!{}_{{\mathfrak{a}}}z_{p^{\infty}{\mathfrak{g}}}=(\,\!_{{\mathfrak{a}}}z_{p^{n}{\mathfrak{g}}})_{n}
is an element of
{\bf H}^{1}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}(1)).

H.3.2.
Zeta morphism

Let
A
be a CM elliptic curve defined over
{\mathbb{Q}}
as in §
H.1
and

{\mathfrak{f}}_{A}
denotes the conductor of the associated Hecke character over
{\mathcal{K}}
.

Fix an element
\gamma\in V_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}
and
put
{\mathfrak{g}}=N_{\mathcal{K}/{\mathbb{Q}}}{\mathfrak{f}}_{A}
.
Let

\!{}_{{\mathfrak{a}}}z_{p^{\infty}{\mathfrak{g}}}^{\gamma}\in{\bf H}^{1}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}(T_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}(1))\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}

denotes the image of the elliptic unit
\!{}_{{\mathfrak{a}}}z_{p^{\infty}{\mathfrak{g}}}

under the composite

{\bf H}^{1}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}(1))\xrightarrow{\otimes\gamma}{\bf H}^{1}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}(1))\otimes V_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\bf H}^{1}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}(V_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}(1))

(cf.
[
49
, Prop. 15.9]
).
If
\gamma\in T_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}
, then

\!{}_{{\mathfrak{a}}}z_{p^{\infty}{\mathfrak{g}}}^{\gamma}\in{\bf H}^{1}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}(T_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}(1)).

Denote by
\!{}_{{\mathfrak{a}}}z^{\gamma,\mathrm{ac}}_{p^{\infty}{\mathfrak{g}}}\in{\bf H}^{1}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\mathrm{ac}}(T_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}(1))

the anticyclotomic projection of
\!{}_{{\mathfrak{a}}}z^{\gamma}_{p^{\infty}{\mathfrak{g}}}

and put

z_{p^{\infty}{\mathfrak{g}},{\mathfrak{a}}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{ac}}=\left(N_{\mathcal{K}/{\mathbb{Q}}}({\mathfrak{a}})-\boldsymbol{\phi}({\mathfrak{a}})^{-1}({\mathfrak{a}},\mathcal{K}[p^{\infty}{\mathfrak{g}}]/\mathcal{K})\right)^{-1}\!_{{\mathfrak{a}}}z_{p^{\infty}{\mathfrak{g}}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{ac}}\in{\bf H}^{1}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\mathrm{ac}}(T_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}(1))\otimes_{\Lambda_{{\mathfrak{g}},{\rm ac}}}Q(\Lambda_{{\mathfrak{g}},\mathrm{ac}}),

where
({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathcal{K}}[p^{\infty}{\mathfrak{g}}]/{\mathcal{K}})\in{\mathrm{Gal}}({\mathcal{K}}[p^{\infty}{\mathfrak{g}}]/{\mathcal{K}})
denotes the associated Artin symbol.

Lemma H.7
.

For
\gamma\in V_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}
,
the element
z_{p^{\infty}{\mathfrak{g}},{\mathfrak{a}}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{ac}}=:z_{p^{\infty}{\mathfrak{g}}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{ac}}
is independent of the choice of
{\mathfrak{a}}
and

z_{p^{\infty}{\mathfrak{g}},{\mathfrak{a}}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{ac}}\in{\bf H}^{1}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\mathrm{ac}}(T_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}(1))\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}.

Moreover, if
\gamma\in T_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}
, then

z_{p^{\infty}{\mathfrak{g}},{\mathfrak{a}}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{ac}}\in{\bf H}^{1}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\mathrm{ac}}(T_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}(1))
.

Proof.

The independence is shown in
[
49
, (15.5.4)]
.

Pick a prime
l\nmid pN_{\mathcal{K}/{\mathbb{Q}}}({\mathfrak{g}})

inert in
\mathcal{K}
such that
l^{3}\not\equiv-1\bmod p
and
put
{\mathfrak{a}}=(l)
. Then we have
N_{\mathcal{K}/{\mathbb{Q}}}({\mathfrak{a}})=l^{2}
and
\boldsymbol{\phi}({\mathfrak{a}})=-l
(cf.
[
Ru
, Lem. 3.1]
).
Since
({\mathfrak{a}},\mathcal{K}[p^{\infty}{\mathfrak{g}}]/\mathcal{K})=1
, it follows
that

N_{\mathcal{K}/{\mathbb{Q}}}({\mathfrak{a}})-\boldsymbol{\phi}({\mathfrak{a}})^{-1}({\mathfrak{a}},\mathcal{K}[p^{\infty}{\mathfrak{g}}]/\mathcal{K})\in\Lambda_{{\mathfrak{g}}}^{\times}

and the assertion follows.
∎

Definition H.8
.

The anticyclotomic zeta morphism for
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}
is defined by

\mathfrak{z}_{{\boldsymbol{\phi}},{\mathfrak{g}}}:\;T_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}\rightarrow{\bf H}^{1}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\mathrm{ac}}(T_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}(1)),\quad\gamma\mapsto z_{p^{\infty}{\mathfrak{g}}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{ac}}.

and its extension to
V_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}
.
Define
\mathfrak{z}_{{\boldsymbol{\phi}}}
to be
the composition of
\mathfrak{z}_{{\boldsymbol{\phi}},{\mathfrak{g}}}
and
the correstriction

{\bf H}^{1}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\mathrm{ac}}(T_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}(1))\rightarrow{\bf H}^{1}_{\mathrm{ac}}(T_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}(1)).

H.3.3.
Explicit reciprocity law

The main result of this subsection is Theorem
H.10
.

As before, we fix embeddings
\iota_{\infty}:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}
and

\iota_{p}:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}_{p}
.
Put
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{K}},(p)}:={\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{K}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{(p)}
and
fix an
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{K}},(p)}
-basis
\gamma_{A}
of
{\mathrm{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{K}},(p)}}(H_{1}(A({\mathbb{C}}),{\mathbb{Z}}_{(p)}),{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{K}},(p)})

and a minimal Weierstrass model of the elliptic curve
A
over
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{K},(p)}
with Néron differential
\omega_{A}\in\mathrm{coLie}(A)
.
We regard
\gamma_{A}

as an
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}
-basis of
T_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}

via the isomorphism

H_{1}(A({\mathbb{C}}),{\mathbb{Z}})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\cong T_{p}(A)
arising from the embedding
\iota_{\infty}
.

Consider the period map

\mathrm{per}:\mathrm{coLie}(A)\to H^{1}(A({\mathbb{C}}),{\mathbb{C}})=\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}(H_{1}(A({\mathbb{C}}),{\mathbb{Q}}),{\mathbb{C}}):\omega\mapsto\left[\;\gamma\mapsto\int_{\gamma}\omega\;\right].

It factors through
the
{\mathbb{C}}
-vector subspace

\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathcal{K}}}(H_{1}(A({\mathbb{C}}),{\mathbb{Q}}),{\mathbb{C}})=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{K}}(H_{1}(A({\mathbb{C}}),{\mathbb{Q}}),{\mathcal{K}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{K}}}{\mathbb{C}},

hence obtains an
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{K}}}
-linear map which we also denote by

\mathrm{per}:\mathrm{coLie}(A)\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{K}},(p)}}(H_{1}(A({\mathbb{C}}),{\mathbb{Z}}_{(p)}),{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{K}},(p)})\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{K}},(p)}}{\mathbb{C}}.

(cf.
[
49
, p. 257]
).
Define
\Omega_{\infty}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}

by

\mathrm{per}(\omega_{A})=\Omega_{\infty}\gamma_{A}.

Consider the complex uniformization
{\mathbb{C}}/\Lambda_{A}\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}A({\mathbb{C}})
such that the pull-back of
\omega_{A}

is
dz
. Then we have

\Lambda_{A}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{(p)}={\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{K}},(p)}\Omega_{\infty}.

By definition, we have the following.

Lemma H.9
.

We have

\mathfrak{z}_{{\boldsymbol{\phi}},{\mathfrak{g}}}({\gamma_{A}})\in{\bf H}^{1}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\mathrm{ac}}(T_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}(1))

and

\mathfrak{z}_{{\boldsymbol{\phi}}}({\gamma_{A}})\in{\bf H}^{1}_{\mathrm{ac}}(T_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}(1))
.

Let
K_{n}
denote the completion of
\mathcal{K}_{n}^{\rm{ac}}
under the embedding
\iota_{p}:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
, and
identify
\mathrm{Gal}(\mathcal{K}_{n}^{\rm{ac}}/\mathcal{K})
with

\mathrm{Gal}(K_{n}/K)
since
h_{\mathcal{K}}=1
.
For a character
\chi:\mathrm{Gal}(\mathcal{K}_{n}^{\rm{ac}}/\mathcal{K})\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\times}
, consider
the composite
