\displaystyle\exp^{*}_{\omega_{A},n}:H^{1}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\boldsymbol{\phi}})\rightarrow H^{1}(K_{n},V_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}(1))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\;\exp^{*}\;}}{{\longrightarrow}}D_{\rm{dR}}^{0}(K_{n},V_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}(1))=K_{n}\omega_{A}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\;\omega_{A}^{\vee}\;}}{{\longrightarrow}}K_{n},

where
\exp^{*}
denotes the dual exponential map for
T_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}(1)
.

We have the following explicit reciprocity law for elliptic units due to Kato (cf.
[
49
, Thm. 15.9]
).

Theorem H.10
.

Let
A
be a CM elliptic curve defined over
{\mathbb{Q}}
and
{\mathcal{K}}
the CM field.
Let
p
be an odd ramified prime and
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}
the associated
p
-adic character of
G_{\mathcal{K}}
.
Then for any character
\chi:{\mathrm{Gal}}({\mathcal{K}}_{n}^{\rm ac}/{\mathcal{K}})\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\times}
, we have
33
33

33

The left and right hand sides are elements of
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
via the embeddings
\iota_{p}
and
\iota_{\infty}
.

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Gal}(K_{n}/K)}\chi(\sigma)\exp^{*}_{\omega_{A},n}(\mathfrak{z}_{{\boldsymbol{\phi}}}(\gamma_{A})^{\sigma})=\frac{L(\overline{\boldsymbol{\phi}}\chi,1)}{\Omega_{\infty}}=\frac{L({\boldsymbol{\phi}}\chi^{-1},1)}{\Omega_{\infty}}.

Remark H.11
.

The above also holds for an inert
p
if we replace
L({\boldsymbol{\phi}}\chi^{-1},1)
by the imprimitive

L
-value
L_{p}({\boldsymbol{\phi}}\chi^{-1},1)
.

H.4.
p
-adic
L
-function

H.4.1.
Elliptic units and local sign decomposition

Let the setting be as in §
H.3
.

For the
p
-adic Hecke character
\boldsymbol{\phi}
,
let
\mathbb{T}_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}
denote the
G_{{\mathcal{K}}}
-representation

T_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}(1)\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}\Lambda^{\iota}
over the anticyclotomic Iwasawa algebra

\Lambda
,
where
\Lambda^{\iota}
denotes the
\Lambda
-free module of rank one with basis
1\in\Lambda

on which
G_{{\mathcal{K}}}
acts by
ga=[g^{-1}]a
for
g\in G_{{\mathcal{K}}}
and
a\in\Lambda.

Then we have a canonical
\Lambda
-module isomorphism

\varprojlim_{m}H^{1}(K_{m},T_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}(1))\cong H^{1}(K,\mathbb{T}_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}),

by which we identify these modules.

Note that
\mathbb{T}_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}
is a conjugate symplectic self-dual
\Lambda
-representation of
G_{K}
.

By the analogue of Rubin’s conjecture as in Theorems
G.29
and
G.36
, if either
p\geq 5
or
p=3
and
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}\equiv\hskip-10.0pt/\ 1\ \mathrm{mod}\ {{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{L}}}
on
G_{K}
, then we have

H^{1}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\boldsymbol{\phi}})=H^{1}_{+}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\boldsymbol{\phi}})\oplus H^{1}_{-}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}),

and if
p=3
and
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}\equiv 1\ \mathrm{mod}\ {{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{L}}}
on
G_{K}
, similarly defining

H^{1}_{\pm}(K,\mathbb{T}_{\boldsymbol{\phi}})=\{x\in H^{1}(K,\mathbb{T}_{\boldsymbol{\phi}})\;|\;\exp^{*}_{\chi}(x)=0\;\text{for all $\chi\in\Xi_{{\boldsymbol{\phi}}|_{G_{K}},n}^{\mp}$}\},

we have an analogous decomposition after tensoring with
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.

Proposition H.12
.

We have

{\mathrm{loc}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathfrak{z}_{{\boldsymbol{\phi}}}(\gamma_{A}))\in H^{1}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{(p)}(\boldsymbol{\phi})}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\boldsymbol{\phi}})\subset H^{1}(K,{\mathbb{T}}_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}),

where

{\varepsilon}^{(p)}(\boldsymbol{\phi}):=\frac{{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{\phi})}{\varepsilon_{p}(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{p}))}.

Proof.

If

\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{\phi}\chi^{-1})=-1
, then

\exp^{*}_{\chi}(\mathfrak{z}_{{\boldsymbol{\phi}}}(\gamma_{A}))=0

by Theorem
H.10
. In the following we relate this global epsilon constant to the local epsilon constant at
p
.

For a finite order anticyclotomic character
\chi
over
{\mathcal{K}}
, we have

\frac{{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{\phi}\chi^{-1})}{{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{\phi})}=\frac{\varepsilon_{p}(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\boldsymbol{\phi}\chi^{-1})}{\varepsilon_{p}(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}({\boldsymbol{\phi}}))}

by Lemma
H.2
i).
So it follows that

{\varepsilon}_{p}(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\boldsymbol{\phi}\chi^{-1}))={\varepsilon}_{p}(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\boldsymbol{\phi}))\cdot\frac{{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{\phi}\chi^{-1})}{{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{\phi})}.

Therefore, the condition
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{\phi}\chi^{-1})=-1
is equivalent to
the condition that

-{\varepsilon}_{p}(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\boldsymbol{\phi}\chi^{-1}))=\frac{{\varepsilon}_{p}(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\boldsymbol{\phi}))}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{\phi})}=:\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{(p)}(\boldsymbol{\phi}).

So the assertion follows from Theorem
G.36
i) and Lemma
H.9
.
∎

Remark H.13
.

i)

If the conductor of
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}
at
{\mathfrak{p}}
equals one, then

\varepsilon^{(p)}({\boldsymbol{\phi}})=\omega_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(-2){\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{\phi})=\left(\frac{-2}{p}\right){\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{\phi})

by Proposition
G.7
i).

ii)

Note that

\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{(p)}(\boldsymbol{\phi})=-\prod_{\ell\nmid p}\varepsilon_{\ell}(\mathrm{Ind}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\boldsymbol{\phi}))
.
Hence, Proposition
H.12
is compatible with
Kato’s global epsilon conjecture for the anticyclotomic
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-deformation of
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}
, which predicts the functional equation

[
NaKato
, Conj. 4.4]
for zeta elements.

H.4.2.
p
-adic
L
-function

Let
\varepsilon
denote the sign of
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{(p)}(\boldsymbol{\phi})
.

In light of Proposition
H.12
we are led to the following bounded
p
-adic
L
-function at primes
p
of additive reduction.

Definition H.14
.

If either
p\geq 5
or
p=3
and
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}\equiv\hskip-10.0pt/\ 1\ \mathrm{mod}\ {{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{L}}}
on
G_{K}
(Case I), then let
v_{\varepsilon}
be a basis of the
\Lambda
-module
H^{1}_{\varepsilon}(K,\mathbb{T}_{\boldsymbol{\phi}})
.
In the excluded case that
p=3
and
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}\equiv 1\ \mathrm{mod}\ {{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{L}}}
on
G_{K}
(Case II),
let
v_{\varepsilon}
be a basis of the
\Lambda\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-module
H^{1}_{\varepsilon}(K,\mathbb{T}_{\boldsymbol{\phi}})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.
Define a
p
-adic
L
-function

\mathscr{L}_{p,v_{\varepsilon}}(A)\in\begin{cases}\Lambda\qquad&(\text{Case I})\\
\;\Lambda\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\qquad&(\text{Case II}).\end{cases}

by

{\mathrm{loc}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathfrak{z}_{{\boldsymbol{\phi}}}(\gamma_{A}))=\mathscr{L}_{p,v_{\varepsilon}}(A)\cdot v_{\varepsilon}.

Note that the
p
-adic
L
-function
\mathscr{L}_{p,v_{\varepsilon}}(A)
also depends on the choice of an ideal
\mathfrak{f}
as in Theorem
H.10
, which we suppress in the notation for simplicity.
For a character
\chi\in\Xi_{{\mathcal{K}}_{\infty}^{\rm ac},\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{+}
, we have

\exp^{*}_{\chi}(v_{\varepsilon})\in D^{0}_{\mathrm{dR}}(K,V_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}(1)(\chi))\subseteq K_{\infty}\otimes_{K}K_{\chi}\omega_{A},

and denote by
\delta^{\omega_{A}}_{v_{\varepsilon}}(\chi)

its image by the projection

K_{\infty}\otimes_{K}K_{\chi}\omega_{A}\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p},\quad a\otimes b\omega_{A}\mapsto ab.

By Corollary
3.2
, we have
\delta^{\omega_{A}}_{v_{\varepsilon}}(\chi)\not=0
.

An interpolation property of
\mathscr{L}_{p,v_{\varepsilon}}(A)
is given by the following.

Theorem H.15
.

Let
A
be a CM elliptic curve defined over
{\mathbb{Q}}
and
{\mathcal{K}}
the CM field.
Let
p
be an odd prime ramified in
{\mathcal{K}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}
the associated
p
-adic character of
G_{\mathcal{K}}
. For a basis
v_{\varepsilon}
of the signed submodule
H^{1}_{\varepsilon}(K,\mathbb{T}_{\boldsymbol{\phi}})
or
H^{1}_{\varepsilon}(K,\mathbb{T}_{\boldsymbol{\phi}})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
as in Definition
H.14
, let
\mathscr{L}_{p,v_{\varepsilon}}(A)
be the associated anticyclotomic
p
-adic
L
-function.
Then for any
\chi\in\Xi_{{\mathcal{K}}_{\infty}^{\rm ac},\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{+}

we have

\chi^{-1}(\mathscr{L}_{p,v_{\varepsilon}}(A))=\frac{1}{\delta_{v_{\varepsilon}}^{\omega_{A}}(\chi)}\cdot\frac{L({\phi}\chi^{-1},1)}{\Omega_{\infty}}.

Proof.

This is a simple consequence of Theorem
H.10
,
noting the commutative diagram

\textstyle{H^{1}(K,\mathbb{T}_{\boldsymbol{\phi}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\chi^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\exp_{\chi}^{*}}
\textstyle{H^{1}(K,T_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}(1)(\chi))=H^{1}(K,T_{{\boldsymbol{\phi}}\chi^{-1}}^{\otimes-1}(1)))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\exp^{*}}
\textstyle{D_{\mathrm{dR}}(K,V_{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{\otimes-1}(1)(\chi)),}

where the horizontal arrow is due to
H^{1}(K,\mathbb{T}_{\boldsymbol{\phi}})\otimes_{\Lambda,\chi^{-1}}K_{\chi}=H^{1}(K,T_{\varphi}^{\otimes-1}(1)(\chi))
.
∎

Remark H.16
.

If the conductor of the Weil–Deligne representation associated to
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}|_{G_{K}}
equals one, then the interpolation region for

\mathscr{L}_{p,v_{\varepsilon}}(A)
may be explicitly stated as consisting of characters

\chi\in\Xi^{\varepsilon}_{\phi|G_{K}}
,
where the local and global
p
-power order anticyclotomic characters are identified.
